# Axis I: Marketing, policies and operations

Detail cards of the master tracker ([`MASTER_IMPLEMENTATION_TRACKER.md`](../MASTER_IMPLEMENTATION_TRACKER.md)), as of 2026-11-23. Requirements: 37. Acceptance criteria: 185 (MET 87, CODED 46, MISSING 52). Code completion 72%, proven by a passing test 47%, release readiness 3%.

Independent verification: done for unit(s) I1, I2.

Every card gives the ten fields of the directive: (1) id and name, (2) functional requirement, (3) what exists in main, (4) what the draft pull requests did, (5) what remains, (6) pull requests and commits, (7) tests that exist and where they ran, (8) dependencies and blockers, (9) acceptance criteria marked MET (a named passing test or CI job on a named head), CODED (code exists, no passing test cited) or MISSING, (10) the next coding task.

#### I1-01. E-mail sender account, shared or separate per store

- **State: CODED (all four criteria met by a passing test; the production mail identity per market is still UNVERIFIED, Q-32)** | plan lines 407 | DECISIONS (safe branding and templates may inherit global defaults; accounts are not inherited across companies); Discovery E06-F06, Q-32 | reviews: security, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Each storefront can send e-mail through the shared account or through its own (host, port, encryption, credentials, from address and name). A message uses the account of the store it belongs to and falls back to the shared one; while the switch is off every Saudi e-mail leaves exactly as it does today.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** One account for the whole install. MailConfigServiceProvider::boot replaces the mail config at every boot from business_settings mail_config (or mail_config_sendgrid when the first is off), from address and name included; EmailTemplateTrait::sendingMail sends with Mail::to(...)->send(). No per-store or per-message account, and no test touches either class.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** Nothing. MailConfigServiceProvider and EmailTemplateTrait are identical to main in the PR 83 and PR 75 trees. PR 48 only makes the abandoned-cart e-mail link name the cart's storefront; the e-mail still leaves through the one account.
- **(5) What remains.** Nothing in code (2026-11-25): the profile table and resolver, the account chosen per send from the market in force rather than at boot, `gcc:notification-readiness`, and now the admin screen with its masked fields, its country scope and its truthful test send. What is left is the owner's: which sender domain belongs to which market and who owns its SPF and DKIM records (Q-32), and whether launch is one shared sender or one per market. An account entered on the screen is read by nothing until `GCC_NOTIFICATION_PROFILES` is on, and the screen says so.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** `wt/bfi`, 2026-11-25: the admin screen, its writer and the test send (`f5b4605e`)
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `local` tests/Feature/Notifications/AdminMailAccountScreenTest (16 tests, 68 assertions), MarketMailAccountTest (15 tests, 39 assertions) and MarketAccountStagingSafetyTest (3 tests, 11 assertions), on SQLite (PHP 8.3.6) and on MariaDB 10.11.14 in this container. Every answer is read out of the `MessageSending` event the real mailer fires, so it is the account the message actually left through
  - `none` No test of MailConfigServiceProvider itself; what it writes at boot is what the per-send account puts back
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Order storefront record (GCC-05 slice 2, PR 87, stacked on PR 64, not in the PR 83 tree). Production mail identity UNVERIFIED (Q-32). A secret-storage pattern exists to reuse: integration_settings credentials are encrypted for Chatwoot (Crypt in ChatwootService) and ZenderSetting.secret is an encrypted cast. EmailTemplateTrait is a shared file (A1-17 reads it too).
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] An e-mail about a KW order leaves through the KW profile's host and from address (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `App\Services\Notification\NotificationProfiles` resolves one account per channel and market out of the additive `notification_channel_profiles` table (`channel`, `country` as `char(2)` `ascii_bin` with `''` for the shared row, plain `settings`, `Crypt`-encrypted `credentials`, `active`, unique on channel+country) -- the storage and the empty-string convention `integration_settings` already uses for the carriers (H1-11). The market is the one IN FORCE, which is the order's own for a message about an order (`OrderNotificationContext`) and the dispatching request's for every other (`ServeInItsStorefront`), so no sender had to be given a country it would otherwise read off `request()` in a worker. Off unless `GCC_NOTIFICATION_PROFILES` is on AND the migration has run, and the table ships empty, so each of the three states -- switch off, no rows, no row for this market -- is the platform of today. `App\Services\Notification\MailAccount::forThisMessage()` puts the market's account in force around the one send and takes it out again, failure included, writing the LEGACY top-level `mail.*` keys because `config/mail.php` of this installation declares them there rather than under `mailers` and Laravel reads the whole `mail` array as the mailer's configuration when `mail.driver` is set -- writing `mail.mailers.smtp.*` instead would have been read by nothing. The built mailer is purged either side, or one built from the previous account would go on being used. It deliberately does NOT touch `mail.from.name`: that field already has an owner, the company that sold the order (`OrderNotificationContext::asTheCompanyThatSold`, A1-17), and two writers of one field is how one of them silently loses; the account decides the host, the credentials and the ADDRESS. `tests/Feature/Notifications/MarketMailAccountTest` (15 tests, 39 assertions): every answer is read at the moment of the send, out of the `MessageSending` event the real mailer fires, because an account applied and put back correctly looks identical from outside. Covered: a Kuwaiti order's host AND from address; a market with no account of its own on the platform's; the shared row used as the fallback with a market's own still winning over it; an account with a host but no from address keeping the platform's address; an account with no host at all changing nothing; an inactive row not used; `kw` not read as `KW`. Teeth: removing the call turns 7 of 15 red. MariaDB 10.11.14 as well as SQLite, and the column was read back off the server: `char(2)` `ascii_bin`, index name 44 characters.)
  - [MET] A queued listener uses the account of the storefront recorded on its order, not the account its worker booted with (BUILT, 2026-11-25. The defect was exactly as the card described it: `MailConfigServiceProvider::boot()` replaces the mail configuration once, at boot, and a worker boots once and then sends one market's messages after another, so the account was the worker's and never the message's. Pinned by `MarketMailAccountTest::test_a_queued_listener_uses_the_account_of_the_market_recorded_on_the_order` -- the request is being served from the Emirati market, which has an account of its own, and the ORDER was sold in Kuwait, so an account read from the request, the session or the boot would be the wrong one and is a different host from the right one -- and by `::test_two_markets_messages_sent_by_one_worker_do_not_inherit_each_others_account`, which sends two orders' mail in one process and asserts the worker is left as it booted, password included, and by `::test_the_platforms_account_is_put_back_when_the_send_fails`.)
  - [MET] A profile's password is stored encrypted and never shown in the admin screen or written to a log (BUILT, 2026-11-25. The third clause was vacuous when this was first written -- there was no admin screen -- and is NOT any more: the screen exists (the criterion below) and `AdminMailAccountScreenTest::test_a_stored_password_is_nowhere_in_the_page` asserts that the whole rendered page does not contain the stored SMTP password, while naming it so an operator can see one is set. What IS built is the answer any such screen must use: `NotificationProfile::visible()` returns the settings and the NAMES of the secrets the account holds, never a value, which is the same answer the carrier settings screen gives. Stored encrypted: `credentials` is `Crypt::encryptString(json)`, and `settings` -- which a screen may show -- is plain JSON, the same split `integration_settings` uses. Tested by `MarketMailAccountTest::test_a_password_is_stored_encrypted_and_the_row_does_not_hold_it` (the plaintext is absent from the stored column and `Crypt::decryptString` returns it), `::test_what_a_screen_may_show_names_the_secrets_it_holds_and_no_value` (the whole `visible()` payload JSON-encoded contains no password, and `credentials_present` is `['username', 'password']`), `::test_a_password_does_not_reach_the_log_when_an_account_cannot_be_read` (the one place that logs about an account -- an undecryptable credential -- logs the channel and nothing else, read out of a Monolog test handler), and `MarketMessagingAccountTest::test_the_readiness_report_prints_no_credential_value`. `MarketAccountStagingSafetyTest` is the mandatory safety test for a new credentials table (3 tests): a restored copy of production is disarmed by the staging plan -- `StagingChannels` now declares this table beside the mail, SMS, Zender, Chatwoot and push sinks, because the senders read it BEFORE the shared settings those neutralise, so disarming them alone would have left a market able to send to real customers from staging. The rows are kept and only `active` and `credentials` cleared, so an operator can still see which markets had accounts. Teeth: unregistering the channel turns all 3 red. The screen is built from `NotificationAccountBoard`, which carries `visible()` and never the profile, so the template cannot print a credential even if it asks for one -- teeth-checked by adding a `value` attribute fed from `credential('password')`, which turns that test red.)
  - [MET] An admin sends a test e-mail through a chosen store profile and sees the real result (BUILT, 2026-11-25, and the requirement is now complete. The screen is `admin/notification-accounts` -- `routes/admin/notification-accounts.php`, registered by `RouteServiceProvider::mapNotificationAccountAdminRoutes()`, behind `module:3rd_party_setup`, which is the permission the one shared mail configuration already sits behind, with a throttle bucket per POST and a tight one on the test send (the only admin action on the platform that opens an outbound SMTP connection to a host and an address the caller just typed). A test send is `App\Services\Notification\MailAccountTestSend`: it runs `MailAccount::forThisMessage(country:)` -- the one new parameter, for the one caller that is asking about a market the admin CHOSE rather than about the market the request is being served from -- round a throwaway `TestEmailSender`, and CATCHES the transport exception to name it rather than swallowing it. `EmailTemplateTrait::writeAndSendMail` is untouched: it catches `Exception` and does nothing with it, which is right for an order confirmation and useless for a credentials test, and changing what 27 classes do with a failed send is a different decision. The answer carries the host and the from address read from INSIDE the send (reading them afterwards reads the platform's, which would tell the operator their test proved something it did not), whether the account was the market's OWN or the shared one, and the server's own refusal. **The secret rules, which are why this was its own slice:** the page is built from `NotificationAccountBoard`, which carries `NotificationProfile::visible()` and so cannot print a credential even if the template asked for one; because the field is therefore blank on load, an empty password field on save KEEPS what is stored and clearing a secret is a separate tick (`forget[]`); and the markets offered are the employee's, resolved with `StaffCountryScope::countriesOf()` rather than `narrow()`, because nothing binds this route -- `narrowLayout()` binds it when the shared layout renders, after the screen has decided what to show -- so a screen that asked `narrow()` would offer every market to a restricted employee. `NotificationAccountWriter` asks the same question again. `tests/Feature/Notifications/AdminMailAccountScreenTest` (16 tests, 68 assertions): the stored password is nowhere in the HTML; a save that changes only the host still authenticates with the stored password; `forget[password]` clears that one and not the username; a saved password is encrypted in the row and absent from `settings`; a test send goes through the Kuwaiti host with the Kuwaiti user, read off `MessageSending`; the platform's account is in force again afterwards, success and failure; a market with no account is told the shared account sent it; the mail server's `535 Authentication failed` reaches the screen with the host that refused; an employee restricted to KW is offered KW and not AE or SA, and may neither write nor test AE; an employee without `3rd_party_setup` is turned away; a save is refused while the switch is off; and a blank field is not stored as an empty setting. Teeth: taking the kept-secret read out of the writer turns 2 red, taking the scope narrowing out of the board turns 3 red, swallowing the transport exception turns 1 red, echoing the password into the form's `value` turns 1 red. NOT covered: only the mail channel can be WRITTEN from this screen -- SMS, WhatsApp and push are shown read-only, because a test SEND for those is a real message to a real number and is a decision of its own; and nothing here asserts what a real SMTP server does.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** None for this requirement. The nearest related work is a test SEND for the SMS, WhatsApp and push accounts, which the screen shows read-only: each of those is a real message to a real number or device and needs a decision about who may trigger one, which is I1-02's remaining question rather than this one's.
- **Open owner decisions.** One shared sender or one per store at launch, and who owns each market's sender domain (SPF, DKIM). Production's mail identity is UNVERIFIED (Discovery Q-32); no owner document answers it.
- **Verifier note (not applied automatically).** deps_blockers corrected by the verifier (applied above).

#### I1-02. SMS, WhatsApp and push profiles per store

- **State: CODED (every criterion met by a passing test; the production accounts per market are still UNVERIFIED, and whether each market gets its own Firebase project is open)** | plan lines 408 | Discovery E06-F06, Q-32, Q-44 | reviews: security, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Each storefront can have its own SMS/OTP gateway, WhatsApp account (Chatwoot inbox and templates, Zender) and push credentials, with the shared profile as fallback. A message uses the profile of the store it belongs to; a number or device of one market does not go through another market's account unless allowed.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** One of each for the whole install. SMSModule and SmsGateway try the gateways in a fixed order and use the first enabled one from the sms_config settings; ZenderOtpService reads ZenderSetting::first(); ChatwootService reads the first enabled integration_settings row 'chatwoot' (one account, one inbox); push uses the single setting push_notification_key (FirebaseServiceProvider builds one singleton from it) and one web-push config.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** Nothing. The PR 83 tree leaves every sender class identical to main. The PR 75 tree changes only PushNotificationService (the Firebase web-worker file generator, PR 63), not which credentials are used. The customer and seller app PRs change no Firebase setting.
- **(5) What remains.** Nothing in code: (1) to (3) and the readiness half of (5) are built and tested (2026-11-25). (4) stands exactly as the card wrote it, and is now recorded in `PushNotificationTrait` itself: an FCM device token belongs to the Firebase project of the app build that registered it, so a market's own credentials reach that market's devices only where that market's build uses that market's project (Q-18, Q-32, A1-17) -- until then a per-market credential for the same project separates quota and audit, not devices. A test SEND, as opposed to a readiness check, needs the admin screen of I1-01.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** `wt/bfi` branch, 2026-11-25 (one commit: the profile table, the resolver, the four senders, the readiness command and the staging declaration)
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `local` tests/Feature/Notifications/MarketMessagingAccountTest (17 tests, 35 assertions: SMS gateway configuration, Zender end to end over the faked HTTP client, the Chatwoot inbox and its approved templates, and the readiness report) and MarketPushAccountTest (6 tests: the Firebase project in the FCM URL), on SQLite (PHP 8.3.6) and on MariaDB 10.11.14 in this container
  - `ci` Password-reset tests assert one OTP text per phone through a faked Zender gateway (the OTP path only, not a per-store choice) (tests/Feature/Security/CustomerPasswordResetTestCase.php and SellerPasswordResetTest.php; PR 75 @47b78ab: Tests SQLite 8.2/8.3, Fulfillment + security MySQL 8 and MariaDB 11.4.2)
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Production values UNVERIFIED: which SMS, Zender, Chatwoot and Firebase accounts exist per market (Q-24, Q-32). Needs the profile resolver of I1-01. A Firebase project change is an app-build decision for the customer and seller apps (Q-18).
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] A KW customer's one-time code goes through the KW SMS profile and an AE customer's through AE's (BUILT, 2026-11-25, on the `notification_channel_profiles` engine I1-01 describes. Both readers the card named are wired. `SMSModule::get_settings()` -- the one input the whole six-gateway chain reads -- answers the market's own account where it has one, and `sendCentralizedSMS` sends such a market through that chain rather than through the published gateway module, which reads the one shared `sms_config` and knows nothing about markets. `ZenderOtpService::send()` builds the market's account as an UNSAVED `ZenderSetting`, so the two senders below it, their refusals and the encrypted `secret` cast all read one kind of object and nothing had to be retyped. **A market's own account is the WHOLE answer for its channel**, and that is the point rather than an edge case: a gateway the market does not name answers nothing and the shared row is not read, because a Kuwaiti one-time code going out through whichever gateway the Saudi account happens to have switched on -- on a sender number registered to another company, in another country, for a message that proves who somebody is -- is the leak this requirement exists to close, and is exactly what a per-gateway fallback would do. `tests/Feature/Notifications/MarketMessagingAccountTest` (17 tests, 35 assertions, SQLite and MariaDB 10.11.14): the Kuwaiti and Emirati twilio accounts read for their own markets; a market with no account reading the shared one; the shared `nexmo` NOT reachable from a market whose own account names only twilio, with the same read proving the shared account really does have it; a market whose gateways are all switched off answering `not_found` rather than sending; and END TO END for Zender, which goes over HTTP and so can be driven -- two codes, two markets, each to its own `base_url` with its own `secret` in the payload, read off the faked request. Teeth: dropping the profile read from `get_settings` turns 3 red; dropping it from `ZenderOtpService` turns 2 red. NOT covered: the five non-Zender gateways send over cURL rather than the HTTP client, so no test asserts the wire for them -- what is asserted is the configuration the chain picks, which is its only input; and `Modules\Gateways\Traits\SmsGateway` is untouched, which is why a market with its own account is routed around it.)
  - [MET] A WhatsApp template send uses the Chatwoot inbox and template of the order's storefront (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `ChatwootService` takes an optional `storefront` and otherwise uses the market in force, resolving `base_url`, `account_id`, `inbox_id` and the access token from that market's account; a market with none reads the one enabled `integration_settings` chatwoot row exactly as before. BOTH halves of the criterion follow from the inbox, which is worth stating: a WhatsApp template is approved per SENDER NUMBER at the provider and the approved list lives ON the inbox (`getWhatsAppTemplates()` reads `message_templates` off `getInbox()`), so reading the market's own inbox is what makes the templates the market's own. Tested in `MarketMessagingAccountTest`: the request really goes to `https://chat.kw.example/api/v1/accounts/71/inboxes/404` with the Kuwaiti token in the `api_access_token` header; a market with no account goes to the shared inbox; the templates offered for a Kuwaiti message are the Kuwaiti inbox's and a Kuwaiti template is NOT offered for another market's; and an account that cannot address a conversation REFUSES rather than falling through to the shared inbox, which for a template another number has not had approved would fail at the provider anyway. Teeth: taking the profile read out of the constructor turns 2 red. NOT covered: `ManualWhatsAppMessageService` and `CartMarketingController` pass no storefront, so they use the market in force, which on an admin screen is the default market -- naming the market on those two screens is part of I1-04's admin work.)
  - [MET] A push to a KW device is sent with the KW push credentials (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `sendNotificationToHttp` reads the market's own service account where it has one and the shared `push_notification_key` otherwise. The project id is IN the FCM URL, so `tests/Feature/Notifications/MarketPushAccountTest` (6 tests) asserts the address the request actually went to rather than a reading of the configuration: a Kuwaiti order's push to `projects/lavkoo-kw`, an Emirati one to the shared project, and the Kuwaiti project again for an order sold in Kuwait while the REQUEST is being served from the Emirates -- the worker criterion for this channel. Each account mints its own token, because the token cache is keyed on `client_email`; two accounts, two token requests. **PUSH FALLS BACK WHERE SMS AND WHATSAPP DELIBERATELY DO NOT, and the reason is recorded in the code:** a market whose account names no service account is sent on the platform's project, because an FCM device token only works with the Firebase PROJECT it was registered in, so refusing would silence that market's pushes and gain nothing -- the safer account does not exist. A shared SMS gateway or WhatsApp inbox is the opposite case: it really would send, from another company's sender number. **AND WHAT A SECOND CREDENTIAL DOES NOT BUY:** for the same reason, a market's own credentials reach that market's devices only where that market's APP BUILD uses that market's project, which is open on A1-17 / Q-18 / Q-32. Until then a per-market credential for the same project separates quota and audit, not devices, and no test here claims a device was reached -- they claim which account the request was made with. Teeth: dropping the profile read turns 3 of 6 red.)
  - [MET] A market with no profile falls back to the shared one, and a missing credential is reported by a readiness check before launch (BUILT, 2026-11-25. **The fallback** is one rule in `NotificationProfiles::for()` -- the market's own active row, else the shared active row, else null, which means the account the platform has always used -- and it is asserted per channel: mail (`MarketMailAccountTest::test_a_market_with_no_account_of_its_own_uses_the_shared_account`, which also pins that a market's own still wins over the shared one), SMS (`::test_a_market_with_no_account_of_its_own_reads_the_shared_gateway` and the Zender pair), WhatsApp (`::test_a_market_with_no_whatsapp_account_of_its_own_uses_the_shared_inbox`) and push (`::test_a_push_about_an_order_of_a_market_with_no_account_of_its_own_goes_out_on_the_shared_project`). An INACTIVE row falls back as though it were absent, and a market written `kw` is not read as `KW`. **The readiness check** is `php artisan gcc:notification-readiness` (`App\Console\Commands\NotificationReadinessCommand`), read-only: for each of the six configured markets and each of the four channels it prints whose account would be used and which required values are missing, and exits 1 when any account a market would send through is incomplete -- so a market that names a mail host but no from address, or a WhatsApp account with no inbox, is found before it opens rather than the first time a customer resets their password. It prints no credential VALUE, only the names of the secrets each account holds. Tested in `MarketMessagingAccountTest`: `MISSING: inbox_id` with exit 1 for a half-written WhatsApp account; the Kuwaiti mail password absent from the report with `credentials present: password` in it; `markets with their own: KW` and `mail no shared account`; every market reading the platform settings on an empty table; and 'off' with exit 0 while the switch is off. NOT covered, and the report says so itself: whether a credential is ACCEPTED by its provider, whether a market's WhatsApp template is approved for its sender number, and whether a market's app build uses that market's Firebase project. Those need a real send, which is the test-send screen of I1-01's last criterion.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** Same branch as I1-01: extend NotificationProfileResolver to sms, whatsapp and push; SMSModule::sendCentralizedSMS, ZenderOtpService::send, the ChatwootService constructor (named argument storefront) and PushNotificationTrait::sendNotificationToHttp read it behind GCC_NOTIFICATION_PROFILES. Tests with Http::fake: URL and token per storefront, FCM token cached per credential, fallback, flag-off identical requests.
- **Open owner decisions.** Whether each market gets its own Firebase project (needs separate app builds) or one shared project with per-market topics (A1-17 lists it as open), and which SMS and WhatsApp accounts each market uses (Q-24, Q-32). No owner document answers either.

#### I1-03. Notification templates: global, with country and language overrides

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 409 | GCC-10; DECISIONS (safe templates may inherit global defaults); Discovery E06-F07, E06-F17 | reviews: native-arabic, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** One global set of notification texts (push messages, e-mail templates, WhatsApp template mappings) with an optional override per country and per language. A send picks country plus language, then language, then the global text, and shows the market's brand, link and currency instead of text fixed at install.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Language overrides exist, country ones do not. email_templates (user_type, template_name) and notification_messages (user_type, key) carry translations by locale; push picks the recipient's app_language, e-mail uses the request language (getDefaultLanguage, so the install default in a queued job, inferred, no test); notification_template_mappings has template_language. No table has a store column. Default e-mail text embeds company_name and url('/'); translated footer and copyright texts are swapped in sendingMail.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** Nothing for templates. The PR 83 tree adds English and Arabic texts for its own new keys only; PR 63 changes the Firebase worker file generator, not message templates.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) override rows keyed by storefront country and locale for notification_messages, email_templates and notification_template_mappings, with the fallback order; (2) templates read the market's brand name, URL and currency at send time (GCC-10, GCC-05); (3) the recipient's language passed to sendingMail and queued listeners; (4) fix the swapped footer and copyright translation; (5) admin screens by country and language; (6) native Arabic review.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#122](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/122) `ce8786b7`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `none` No test of EmailTemplate translation, the template service, NotificationMessage lookup or the WhatsApp mapping in main, PR 83 or PR 75
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** GCC-10 brand per country (unit F) supplies name, logo and URL; the order's storefront record (GCC-05 slice 2, PR 87, stacked on PR 64) decides which override applies; WhatsApp templates need per-sender approval at the provider. Texts in the database are production data, UNVERIFIED.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MISSING] A KW order's push text comes from the KW override, else the recipient-language text, else the global message (no storefront column on notification_messages or email_templates)
  - [CODED] A push body is in the recipient's app_language when a translation exists, else the global message (lavkoo PR 122 (draft, base main, head ce8786b7, 7 test files and one CI group, no application file): tests/Feature/Notifications/PushBodyLanguageTest (14 tests) fires the real OrderStatusEvent through the real OrderStatusListener with Google's token endpoint and FCM faked at the HTTP boundary, and reads the FCM request that would have left the server. CI run 367 (pull_request, head ce8786b7, 23 jobs, 17 success): the new group "Notification and localization tests" on MySQL 8 and on MariaDB 11.4.2 (37 tests, 174 assertions, success, no skips on either; the group's three directories exist only on this branch and hold its four classes, 14+12+5+6=37) and the whole suite on SQLite 8.2 and 8.3 (6268 tests, 97 skipped, one failure, TorodShipmentDiscoveryTest, which is red on main; this class is neither skipped nor red on any engine). Asserted for each of the four recipients of sendOrderNotification (customer, seller, delivery man, promoter): exactly one push leaves the server, to that recipient's own device token, and notification.body and the data copy carry the recipient's own translation row when their app_language has one and the message's global text when it has none; for the customer also the store default language's row when the customer has no app_language and that language has one, the global text when neither the customer's language nor the default has one, and the global text rather than the default language's row when the customer's own language has none; for the seller, the delivery man and the promoter the recipient's own language rather than the referring customer's, whose app_language is set to a language that does have a row. Audit mutations on that head (baseline 14 tests, 70 assertions, green): returning the global text instead of the translation row turns 5 red; eager-loading a translation without the recipient's locale turns 10 red; ignoring the customer's app_language turns 2 red; the seller branch taking the customer's app_language turns 2 red. The hand-built notification_messages table of the test matches the live migration 2023_10_07_182714. Not covered: the title (translate() in the language of the request, not the recipient's); the rest of the payload (type, order_id, message_key, deliveryman_charge) and the deliveryman_notifications row; a message with status 0, which sends nothing; the chat, wallet-withdrawal, cash-collection, customer-block, product-request and admin fund-added pushes, which share pushNotificationMessage but take the language from their own caller — no test drives any of them, and the withdraw dispatcher (VendorController:775) passes the delivery man's app_language for a push sent to the seller's token; and no country or storefront override exists on notification_messages to test.)
  - [MET] A translated e-mail shows its own footer and its own copyright text (FIXED, 2026-11-25. The defect was real and user-visible: `EmailTemplateTrait::sendingMail` gave `footer_text` the `copyright_text` translation row and `copyright_text` the `footer_text` row, so every recipient whose language had a translation was shown the copyright notice in the footer slot of the e-mail and the "contact us for any queries" line at the foot of the page. The admin's own preview of the same template reads the two by their own keys (resources/views/admin-views/business-settings/email-template/index.blade.php:12), so the screen the wording is approved on did not show what was sent. The five assignments are now one lookup against a list of the fields an e-mail renders, so the field a row reaches is the row's own key and cannot be anything else -- the bug class is gone, not only this instance. `tests/Feature/Storefront/EmailTemplateFooterTest` (3 tests, 15 assertions) drives the real queued listener and the real mailer and reads the two slots out of the HTML that would have left the server, by the classes the design gives them (`view-footer-text` above the signature, `view-copyright-text` at the foot): the Arabic footer and the Arabic copyright each in their own slot; a recipient whose language has no row shown the stored English of both; and a language that translates only the footer shown its own footer with the STORED copyright, never the other's. Teeth: before the fix 2 of the 3 were red with the swapped Arabic printed; after it, dropping `copyright_text` from the list turns 1 red. Siblings re-run green on the same tree: OrderNotificationStorefrontTest 26, OrderNotificationTodayTest 7, MarketSenderIdentityTest 5. NOT covered: the designs other than order-place, and the `button_name` and `body` rows, which were already correct and are unchanged.)
  - [MET] A queued e-mail renders in the recipient's language, not the worker's default (2026-11-25, and **HALF OF THIS CARD WAS ALREADY STALE**. An e-mail ABOUT AN ORDER has been written in its recipient's language since I1-06: `OrderNotificationContext::within` reads the order's own customer, seller or delivery man and binds that language around the send, and the template read is inside the binding, so `translationCurrentLanguage` -- which filters at QUERY time by `getDefaultLanguage()` -- reads the recipient's rows. Already proven by `OrderNotificationStorefrontTest::test_a_queued_order_email_is_written_in_the_language_of_its_recipient` (:518), `::test_the_order_received_email_of_a_vendor_is_written_in_the_language_the_vendor_reads` (:185) and `::test_the_digital_product_download_email_is_written_in_the_language_of_the_buyer` (:311). The quoted evidence "sendingMail takes no language" was true of the OTHER half and of nothing else. **What was really missing, and is now built:** every e-mail that is about no order -- registration, verification, password reset, wallet top-up, restock notice, 11 queued listeners in all -- reached `sendingMail()` with nothing but an address, so it was written in the install default, or in whatever language the job before it left in the worker's session. `App\Services\Notification\MessageLanguage` now answers the one question "what language is this message written in" for both halves: `write()` binds the locale AND the session language and puts both back (the body `OrderNotificationContext::inLanguage` used to hold, now delegated to it, so there is one chooser and not two), and `asTheRecipientReads()` looks the recipient's own `app_language` up in the row that holds their address -- `users`, `sellers` or `delivery_men`, through the query builder, so neither user model nor any global scope is involved -- and leaves alone any language the order path has already chosen. Read from the STORED ROW, never from `request()`: the request that raised the event may belong to an administrator topping up somebody else's wallet or to a payment gateway calling back. Guarded by `Schema::hasColumn`, memoized on a `#[Singleton]` instance rather than statically, so an installation without the column and a test with its own tables are each answered about their own schema. Gated on `gcc.storefronts.enabled`, the same switch the order half already runs behind, so a one-market platform is byte for byte unchanged. `tests/Feature/Storefront/RecipientLanguageEmailTest` (9 tests, 18 assertions): a wallet top-up e-mail in the recipient's Arabic; a recipient who chose no language written to in the store language; the switch OFF leaving it in the store language; an address that belongs to nobody; an ORDER e-mail to a guest address still Arabic, which can only have come from the order; a message to platform staff staying in the platform's language; the locale and the session both put back after a send and after a failure; and a language already chosen not chosen again. Teeth: removing the CALL from `sendingMail` (not the helper) turns 2 red -- the Arabic title and the session language that then leaks to the next message. Re-run green on this tree: OrderNotificationStorefrontTest 26, OrderNotificationTodayTest 7, MarketSenderIdentityTest 5, EmailTemplateFooterTest 3, MarketLanguageTest 6, MarketingStopAbandonedCartTest 39, NecessaryNotificationsTest 8, AbandonedCartConsentTest 16, MarketingConsentDormancyTest 8, AbandonedCartReminderLedgerTest 5, CustomerPasswordResetWebTest 23, SellerPasswordResetTest 62. NOT covered: an admin recipient has no `app_language` of their own to read and is deliberately left in the platform's language; SMS and WhatsApp bodies, which take their language from their own callers; and a message sent to several addresses at once, which has no single recipient and stays in the sender's language.)
  - [MET] Default templates carry no market-specific company name or URL (FIXED, 2026-11-25. `EmailTemplateService` baked three facts into the row at the moment it was CREATED -- `getWebConfig('company_name')` 11 times, `url('/')` 14 times and `date('Y')` once -- and a template row is created once, the first time its screen is opened, then read by every market and every year afterwards. So a platform that was renamed went on signing e-mails with its old name, one that moved went on linking to its old address, the copyright line stayed on whichever year the row happened to be written, and EVERY market's e-mail sent its reader to the DEFAULT market's website: a Kuwaiti customer's registration e-mail invited them to the Saudi shop. All three are placeholders in the default text now -- `{companyName}`, `{websiteUrl}`, `{currentYear}` -- resolved when the message is written, in `EmailTemplateTrait::brandOfThisMessage()`, over the five fields an e-mail renders. The address comes from `StorefrontUrl::of()`, which answers for the storefront IN FORCE, so a message about an order takes the market the order was sold in (bound by `OrderNotificationContext`) and every other message the market of the request that raised it (carried to the worker by `ServeInItsStorefront`): no new parameter had to be threaded anywhere. The default market and a platform with storefronts off keep the bare address they have today, and a stored text holding none of the three placeholders -- which is every row an existing installation already has -- is sent byte for byte as it is stored. Also corrected while in the same statements: six default bodies read "visit theLavkoo shop website" with no space, which was in the original string concatenation. `tests/Feature/Storefront/EmailTemplateBrandTest` (7 tests, 158 assertions): the stored registration default names no company and no address and does hold all three placeholders; a scan of ALL 17 default templates of all four recipient types over title, body, footer and copyright finds neither fact in any of them; a renamed platform's e-mail says the new name in the sentence the placeholder sits in (not merely somewhere in the page -- the design also prints the name in its signature, so a looser assertion would pass on the signature alone); a Kuwaiti order's e-mail links to `/kw`; a Saudi order's and, with storefronts off, a Kuwaiti one's link to the bare address; and a template with no placeholders is sent as stored. Teeth: removing the resolution CALL turns 4 of 7 red; baking the company name back into one default turns 3 red. Siblings re-run green: EmailTemplateFooterTest 3, RecipientLanguageEmailTest 9, OrderNotificationStorefrontTest 26, OrderNotificationTodayTest 7, MarketSenderIdentityTest 5. NOT covered, and deliberately: `{companyName}` resolves to the platform's ONE marketing name in every market, because a brand a market may override is F-13 and has no record to read yet -- this method is the single place that changes when it does; the e-mail DESIGNS also print `getWebConfig('company_name')` directly in their signature and header, which is read at render time and so is not baked, but is likewise the global name until F-13; and no row an installation already holds is rewritten, because that is production data.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** Test-first on a branch cut from main: tests/Feature/Notifications/EmailTemplateLanguageTest (Mail::fake, in-memory tables) pinning today's output and the swapped footer and copyright as a failing-then-fixed test. Then the recipient locale as a named argument of sendingMail and the override tables behind GCC_NOTIFICATION_TEMPLATES; add tests/Feature/Notifications to the Fulfillment + security CI paths.
- **Open owner decisions.** Which texts must differ per country (brand, currency, legal wording, Gulf Arabic) and who approves each variant. No owner document answers it; DECISIONS only allows inheritable branding defaults.
- **Verifier note (not applied automatically).** deps_blockers corrected by the verifier (applied above).
- **Update after the assessment.**  PR 122 (draft, tests and one CI group, ce8786b7, main based; CI run 367: 17 of 23 jobs, the rest are the overlay and the Torod test of main): the body of an order push is read from the recipient's own translation row for the customer, the seller, the delivery man and the promoter (14 tests); the title is in the language of the request, not of the recipient, and chat, wallet, cash-collect, withdraw, block and product-request pushes are not driven. Not audited: no state changes here.

#### I1-04. Marketing audiences are isolated by store

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 410 | DECISIONS (marketing consents have correct scopes; notifications market-scoped); Discovery E06-F08 | reviews: owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A marketing message (push broadcast, in-app notification, newsletter, WhatsApp cart marketing, abandoned-cart e-mail) names its store or stores and reaches only the customers of those stores. No list, topic or inbox spans markets by accident, and an employee restricted to a country can target only that country.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** No audience has a store. The admin broadcast saves a notifications row (title, text, image) with no store and pushes it to the topic sixvalley and to every approved seller token; GET notifications returns every sent_to=customer row to every customer. subscriptions holds an e-mail only. CartMarketingController lists every registered customer with a cart and a phone. Abandoned-cart reminders are chosen from all carts, each about its recipient's own cart.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 48 (in PR 83): the signed abandoned-cart link names the cart's storefront (AbandonedCartService::recoveryUrl) and the recovery redirect keeps it; carts are isolated by storefront (StorefrontCartScope). No broadcast, in-app list, newsletter, WhatsApp list or admin screen gained a store, and no staff-scope rule mentions NotificationController or CartMarketingController.
- **(5) What remains.** Items (1) for `notifications`, (2), (4), (5) and (6) are built and tested (2026-11-25): the markets column, the admin multi-select narrowed by the employee's country scope, the app inbox and its unread counter, the per-market topic and eleven tests. What is left: the NEWSLETTER (`subscriptions` still holds one column, `email`), the WhatsApp cart-marketing list and send, and the experience-campaign screens. The seller half of a broadcast -- it also pushes to every approved seller's device -- names no market, and which sellers a market's broadcast concerns is an open question rather than an oversight: a seller works in a shop, not in a market.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#48](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/48) `4a821d3`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`; `wt/bfi`, 2026-11-25: the markets of a broadcast, its topic and the app inbox
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `local` tests/Feature/Notifications/BroadcastMarketTest (11 tests, 24 assertions: the stored markets, the topics published to, the app inbox of two markets, the unread counter, the resend and the restricted employee), on SQLite (PHP 8.3.6) and on MariaDB 10.11.14 in this container
  - `ci` StorefrontRecoveryLinkTest (5): the reminder link of a cart made in another country names that storefront and stays signed, opens that storefront's cart, and is unchanged while storefronts are off (tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontRecoveryLinkTest.php; PR 83 @8ff3410: Tests SQLite 8.2/8.3, Storefront tests MySQL 8 and MariaDB 11.4.2)
  - `none` No test of the newsletter or the WhatsApp cart-marketing list by store
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Staff country scope for these screens is GCC-14c slice 4, not started (unit G); topic naming is I1-05; consent is I1-08 and A2-13. Production audience sizes UNVERIFIED.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] The link in an abandoned-cart e-mail opens the storefront the cart was made in, signed, and is unchanged while storefronts are off (StorefrontRecoveryLinkTest::test_the_link_of_a_cart_made_in_another_country_names_that_storefront_and_stays_signed and 4 more; PR 83 @8ff3410: SQLite 8.2/8.3, Storefront MySQL 8, MariaDB 11.4.2)
  - [MET] An admin broadcast can be limited to chosen stores, and another store's app inbox does not list it (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `notifications.storefront_countries` (`2026_12_30_100000_add_markets_to_notifications_table`, `text`, nullable, read back off MariaDB 10.11.14 as `text null` after `sent_to`; `up()` idempotent, `down()` reverses it) holds the markets ONE broadcast is for, as a JSON list, because the requirement is one, several or all markets and two broadcasts for two markets would show twice to a customer who reads both. **An unmarked row means EVERY market, not the default market's** -- the opposite of the convention `StorefrontMarketRows` uses, and the reason is the difference between a message and a setting: an unmarked shipping rule was WRITTEN for the only storefront there has ever been, an unmarked broadcast was SENT to everybody and still is, so every row an installation already has keeps reaching every customer. `App\Services\Notification\BroadcastMarkets` answers all four questions a broadcast asks about markets in one place -- which an operator may choose, what is stored, which topics it is published to, and which inbox lists it -- because a broadcast stored for Kuwait and published platform-wide is the worst of both answers. The admin form gained a multi-select (`resources/views/admin-views/notification/index.blade.php`), the list a Markets column, and the app inbox (`RestAPI\v1\NotificationController::list`) is narrowed to the broadcasts that name this market and the ones that name none -- with the UNREAD COUNTER narrowed the same way, because a badge of three over a list of two is how a customer learns to ignore the badge. The route group gained the `storefront` middleware, without which the app's `X-Storefront` header never reached the controller and the reader could narrow nothing -- the trap EXECUTION_STATE records for three other controllers. `tests/Feature/Notifications/BroadcastMarketTest` (11 tests, 24 assertions, SQLite and MariaDB 10.11.14): with storefronts off a broadcast names no market and goes to `sixvalley`; a Kuwaiti broadcast stores `["KW"]`; the KW inbox lists the KW and the unmarked broadcast and NOT the Emirati one, and the AE inbox the mirror of that; the counter counts the rows the list shows. Teeth: removing the narrowing call from the API controller turns 2 red, and so does removing the `storefront` middleware from the route -- the second is the one a test of the service alone would have missed. NOT covered: the broadcast also pushes to every approved SELLER's device token (`sendPushNotificationToMultipleDevices`), which names no market and is not narrowed here; a seller works in a shop, not in a market, and which sellers a market's broadcast concerns is a question this slice does not answer, so it is left as it is and named here rather than guessed at.)
  - [MET] A newsletter signup records its storefront and a send reaches only that storefront's subscribers (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `subscriptions` held an e-mail address and nothing else, so six markets shared one list and the only thing an operator could do with it was send to all of it. `2026_12_31_100000_add_storefront_country_to_subscriptions` adds the market (additive, nullable, `char(2)` `ascii_bin`, run twice against MariaDB 10.11.14 and idempotent, `down()` reversible twice over), `App\Services\Notification\NewsletterMarkets` answers the three questions once, and `App\Models\Scopes\NewsletterMarketScope` narrows the admin list AND its export by one rule on the model -- a scope rather than an argument because both go through the same repository method and **an export that forgets is a file of every market's addresses leaving the building**, and an Eloquent scope rather than a `where` because the subscriber search ends in an `orWhere` on the e-mail. **The duplicate check had to move with the list and that is the part that was a defect**: it was GLOBAL, so a Kuwaiti visitor whose address was already on the Saudi list was told they had already subscribed and no Kuwaiti row was written. NULL is the DEFAULT storefront's, not every market -- the opposite of the broadcast convention, because an unmarked broadcast was SENT to everybody while an unmarked subscriber signed up on the only storefront there has ever been -- so a send to Saudi Arabia reaches exactly the people it reaches today. `subscriptions` leaves the register's `inherited` bucket for `{scope: storefront, by: storefront_country}`. `NewsletterMarketTest` (tests/Feature/Marketing/NewsletterMarketTest.php): 11 green on MariaDB 10.11.14; Marketing, Localization, TableOwnershipTest and GccMigrationsContractTest 174 green together. Teeth: not recording the market turns 4 red, the global duplicate check 2 red, the screen not binding its market 3 red, the default storefront not owning the unmarked rows 1 red. What this does NOT claim: that a newsletter SENDER exists. Nothing in the application sends to this list -- `NewsletterConsentTest::test_no_sender_in_the_application_reads_the_newsletter_list` pins that, and now also pins that the one reader carries no send -- so what is proved is that the list an operator exports is one market's.)
  - [MET] The WhatsApp cart-marketing list shows only customers with carts in the chosen storefront (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `App\Services\Notification\CartMarketingMarkets` gives the screen a market chooser, narrowed to the operator's own markets by `StaffCountryScope`, and **the list and the send ask the same object the same question** -- the form carries customer ids, so a list narrowed to Kuwait over a send that is not narrowed is one hand-written POST away from reaching the market it was narrowed out of, and the test posts exactly that id. No market chosen means every market the operator may reach, which for an unrestricted one is the screen of today and for a restricted one is their own markets, never the platform. A cart group spanning two markets is left out of a narrowed list rather than half-messaged, because the message carries the whole cart's total; a group whose rows name no country stays visible only to an operator who may reach every market. `CartMarketingMarketTest` (tests/Feature/Marketing/CartMarketingMarketTest.php): 11 green on MariaDB 10.11.14, Marketing group 141 green. Teeth: dropping the list's narrowing turns 2 red, the send's 2 red, and making a restricted operator's `inForce()` return null -- the "nothing chosen means everybody" hole -- turns 3 red. The test also records an interaction rather than fighting it: Kuwait's cart is withheld even with Kuwait chosen, because Kuwait may not sell yet and I1-07's marketing stop holds it, so the narrowing's own half is proved on the service where the selling gate is not in the way.)
  - [MET] An employee restricted to KW can target only KW in a broadcast or campaign (COMPLETE, 2026-11-25, across the three screens the criterion and its predecessor named. BROADCAST (2026-11-25, earlier): `BroadcastMarkets::choices()` offers a restricted employee their own markets and `accepted()` guarantees a restricted employee's broadcast always names one or more of them, whatever the form carried -- the hole found there was that an empty intersection read as "every market", so a Kuwaiti employee naming the Emirates would have broadcast PLATFORM-WIDE; `BroadcastMarketTest`. CAMPAIGN: `App\Services\Commerce\CampaignMarkets` applies the same rule to the experience campaigns, and `CampaignController` refuses an employee whose scope grants no market, narrows what they are offered, and refuses them a campaign of a market that is not theirs -- a platform-wide one included, which is refused rather than silently confined to their market; `CampaignMarketTest` 16 green. CART MARKETING: the WhatsApp screen above. Teeth on each: dropping the scope read from `BroadcastMarkets::choices()` turns 4 red, from `CampaignMarkets::choices()` 4 red, and the cart-marketing fallback 3 red.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** The newsletter: a nullable `storefront_country` on `subscriptions`, written from the storefront in force at signup, and a send narrowed to it -- the same shape as the broadcast, except that a subscription belongs to ONE storefront (the one the visitor signed up on) rather than to a list, so `StorefrontMarketRows`' convention applies to it and an unmarked row IS the default market's. Then the WhatsApp cart-marketing list.
- **Open owner decisions.** How a broadcast chooses its audience: one store, several or all, and whether 'all' stays possible for the platform owner. No owner document answers it (Discovery E06-F08).

#### I1-05. Push topics do not overlap between markets

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 418 | GCC-20 document (admin topics must not reuse customer topics); Discovery E06 section 2.2, Q-32, Q-44; A1-17 | reviews: owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Push topics carry the market, so a broadcast reaches one country and no device is subscribed to two markets' marketing topics. The apps subscribe to the topic of the chosen storefront and leave the one they left; the names Saudi apps hold today keep working; admin and seller topics never reuse customer topics.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Global topics only. Admin broadcasts go to the default topic sixvalley; the website subscribes to sixvalley and maintenance_mode_start_user_app; the customer app subscribes to sixvalley (as topic and demoTopic), maintenance_mode_start_user_app and theme_updates_user_app and never unsubscribes; the theme publish beacon goes to one topic; restock topics are per request and product. No topic name carries a market, language or audience.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 48 (in PR 83): a restock request for a product only another storefront sells is refused and creates nothing, so restock topics stay with the product's own country. No PR changes a topic name or an app subscription: customer app PR 7 clears caches on a storefront change and touches no topic.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) is built (2026-11-25): `StorefrontPushTopics::customerMarketing()` names a market's marketing topic, `sixvalley` stays the platform-wide one, and the admin broadcast publishes to the topics of the markets it is for. (3) is answered and documented (the beacons are global, with the reason measured). The website half of (2) is built, the APPS' half is not -- the customer app still subscribes to one topic and never unsubscribes, which is axis J's work in lavkooapp. (4) and the seller topic are open on Q-44 and GCC-20.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#48](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/48) `4a821d3`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`; [lavkoo#122](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/122) `ce8786b7`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` StorefrontCartEdgesTest: a restock request for a product only another storefront sells is refused and creates nothing (tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontCartEdgesTest.php; PR 83 @8ff3410: Tests SQLite 8.2/8.3, Storefront tests MySQL 8 and MariaDB 11.4.2)
  - `none` No test in the backend or the customer app names or asserts a topic
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Owner choice of one topic or one per market. The app change is a customer-app release (unit J); the seller topic lavkoo_seller has no sender (Q-44, unit K); the broadcast target depends on I1-04.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [CODED] A restock topic subscription exists only for a product the request's storefront sells (StorefrontCartEdgesTest::test_a_restock_request_for_a_product_only_another_storefront_sells_is_refused_and_creates_nothing; PR 83 @8ff3410: SQLite 8.2/8.3, Storefront MySQL 8, MariaDB 11.4.2)
  - [MET] Each market has its own marketing topic, and a broadcast to KW is published to KW's topic only (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `StorefrontPushTopics::customerMarketing(country:)` names it, beside the two maintenance topics that class already named, so the server and the client build one name in ONE place -- a mismatch between a publisher and a subscriber is silent, the message goes nowhere and nobody is told. `CUSTOMER_MARKETING` is `sixvalley`, the name the published platform shipped with, and it is deliberately NOT renamed: it is what the installed app builds already subscribe to, so renaming it would silence every device until it updated. A market's topic is that name with its code appended, and the platform-wide topic still carries a broadcast that names no market, so an app build older than the market topics is not cut off. The admin broadcast and its resend publish once per market the broadcast is for (`BroadcastMarkets::topicsFor()`), in the order the markets are CONFIGURED rather than the order the form listed them, because the stored list and the topics published to are one list and an order that depends on which box was ticked first is one two readings can disagree on; a resend repeats the markets the broadcast was SENT with, not the ones the person resending it could choose. `getFCMTopicListToSubscribe()` adds the market's topic for the browser, keeping the two platform-wide names on the list untouched. `tests/Feature/Notifications/BroadcastMarketTest` reads the topic off the request that reached FCM: a Kuwaiti broadcast goes to `sixvalley_KW` ALONE; a broadcast for two markets to `sixvalley_AE` and `sixvalley_KW` and to no platform-wide topic; one that names no market to `sixvalley`, as before; and with storefronts off to `sixvalley` whatever the form said. `PushTopicNamesTest` stays green, with its pinned list of campaign-push arguments updated on purpose -- that list exists so that a changed topic is a decision made on it. Teeth: publishing to the platform-wide topic regardless of the markets turns 5 red. NOT covered: whether a DEVICE receives it. A device receives a market's topic only once its app build subscribes to that topic, which is the criterion below and is axis J's work in lavkooapp; what is asserted here is the name the server published to, which is the half that has to be right first.)
  - [MISSING] The customer app subscribes to its storefront's topic and unsubscribes from the previous one when the storefront changes (auth_repository subscribes to AppConstants.topic only; the app has no unsubscribe call)
  - [CODED] The topics Saudi apps already hold keep receiving messages (lavkoo PR 122 (draft, base main, head ce8786b7, tests and one CI group only: 7 test files and .github/workflows/ci.yml, no application file): tests/Feature/Notifications/PushTopicNamesTest, 12 tests. CI run 367 (pull_request, head ce8786b7): the new group "Notification and localization tests" on MySQL 8.0.46 and MariaDB 11.4.2 (tests/Feature/Notifications, Retention and Localization: 37 tests, 174 assertions, OK, zero skips, PHP 8.3.36) and the whole suite on SQLite 8.2 and 8.3 (6268 tests, 97 skipped, one failure, TorodShipmentDiscoveryTest, red on main); this class is in no skip list and no failure list of any job, and no base class of it can skip. Six behaviour tests read the topic of the message at the faked FCM call, through the real publishers: theme_updates_user_app from ThemeSyncBeacon::announce; maintenance_mode_start_user_app, maintenance_mode_start_vendor and maintenance_mode_start_deliveryman, in that order, from the real admin maintenance switch BusinessSettingsController::updateSystemMode; sixvalley from a campaign push that names no topic and again from the real admin NotificationController add and resend; demo_reset from the trait's demo-reset alert; restock_7_product_restock_9_topic from ProductService::sendRestockProductNotification. A seventh test asserts the list getFCMTopicListToSubscribe() returns — sixvalley, maintenance_mode_start_user_app and the customer's session restock topics — which is the list handed to the browser, not a message sent. Five structural tests scan app, Modules, routes and config (each guarded by assertGreaterThan(500) on the file count): every listed topic still written in the file the list names and the three blade pages still calling the helper; no topic literal written that the list does not know; the files that call sendNotificationToHttp or name fcm.googleapis.com; the value of the topic key of every message those senders build; the arguments of every sendPushNotificationToTopic and subscribeToTopic call. Audit mutations on that head: renaming the vendor maintenance topic turns the maintenance wire test and two scans red; renaming the sixvalley default of the trait turns both campaign wire tests (one through the real controller) and two scans red; a theme beacon that returns before sending, and a helper that stops merging the session restock topics, each turn only their own behaviour test red while the scans stay green. Not covered: the apps themselves, which is the half of this criterion about the topics Saudi apps hold — the customer app keeps sixvalley (as topic and as demoTopic), maintenance_mode_start_user_app and theme_updates_user_app and the seller app keeps lavkoo_seller and maintenance_mode_start_vendor in their own constants, and no test in any of the three repositories reads those constants or compares them with this list; a message built without a literal topic key or reaching FCM through another call; the rendered browser page (the three blade pages are a text scan only); and whether any device receives anything, since the FCM call is faked.)
  - [MET] A maintenance or theme-update beacon of one market does not wake another market's apps, or is documented as global (2026-11-25, on the criterion's SECOND limb, deliberately and with the reason measured. **Scoping the theme topic would have been worse than leaving it global.** Every customer app subscribes to `theme_updates_user_app` at startup, before login, so a suffix per market would silence a market until its own app build subscribed to its own topic -- an app release away (A1-17, Q-18, Q-32). A wasted wake is a far smaller cost than a market whose phones never hear a publish, so the topic stays one name and the globality is now DOCUMENTED, in `ThemeSyncBeacon`'s own docblock, with what keeps it harmless and what it costs. Two things keep it harmless, and both are asserted rather than argued: the beacon carries no theme content, so a woken client asks `/theme/version` for ITS OWN storefront and is answered that storefront's revision; and the payload now NAMES the market a publish was for, so a client can ignore a beacon that is not its own instead of comparing another market's revision number against its own. The market key is additive and absent while storefronts are off, the same shape as the market a push about an order carries. **The maintenance beacons are global for a stronger reason**: the maintenance they announce IS platform-wide -- one `system_maintenance_mode` entry stops the whole platform and the switch chooses which APP is told, never which market -- so a per-market maintenance topic would announce a per-market state that does not exist. `tests/Feature/Notifications/BeaconsThatWakeEveryMarketTest` (8 tests, 25 assertions): a Kuwaiti publish is announced ONCE on the one global topic; a Saudi app woken by it is told exactly what it was told before (the whole /api/v1/theme/version answer compared, not only the revision), which is the cost of the global topic stated and bounded; the Kuwaiti app is told the revision the beacon announced; the beacon names KW; a publish of the DEFAULT storefront's theme names no market, because every market without a theme of its own falls back to it; with storefronts off the data payload is byte for byte `['type', 'revision']`; the payload still carries no theme content and no notification block; and the maintenance switch is read from its own source and shown to write one platform-wide entry, to name no market anywhere, and to name its topics per app. Teeth: dropping the `storefronts` argument at the publish turns 1 red. Neighbours green on this tree: PushTopicNamesTest 12, StorefrontThemePublishTest 10, StorefrontThemeServingTest 11, StorefrontThemeScheduledPublishTest 51, StorefrontThemeActivationTest 7, PlatformMaintenanceModeTest 28, StorefrontMaintenancePushTest 12. NOT covered: whether the customer app READS the market key -- it does not yet, which is the criterion below about the app's subscriptions and is axis J's work; until it does, the key is a capability the backend offers and nothing reads.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** After the owner picks one topic or one per market: on the PR 64 head add StorefrontTopics::marketing(country) ('sixvalley' for SA, a suffix for others, off by default); use it in Admin NotificationController::add and resend and in getFCMTopicListToSubscribe; in lavkooapp PR 7's branch subscribe and unsubscribe in the storefront-change notifier. Tests: name per storefront, SA unchanged, flag-off identical, Dart test with a fake messaging client.
- **Open owner decisions.** One topic or one per market (A1-17 records it as open; no owner document answers it); whether the seller topic lavkoo_seller gets a sender (Discovery Q-44); which Firebase project each app uses (Q-32).
- **Update after the assessment.**  PR 122 (ce8786b7, run 367): the list of push topics and their names, scans that fail on a renamed topic or a new sender, and the wire call of the admin campaign and the maintenance switches (12 tests); the customer app's side is not covered. Not audited: no state changes here.

#### I1-06. Order notifications are built from the store that created the order

- **State: NOT STARTED** | plan lines 411 | GCC-13; DECISIONS (notifications market-scoped); Discovery E06-F07, E06-F16, E06-F17 | reviews: none
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A notification about an order (placed, status change, refund, edit, due payment, referral reward) is built from the order's own store: sender account, template, language, currency of amounts, link and topic. A queued job serves the storefront of its order and never takes these from whichever process happens to send it.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Orders carry no storefront on main. OrderPlacedListener is queued; OrderStatusListener and RefundListener run inline. They build the push from the recipient's app_language or the default language, take the title from translate('order') in the request's language, send e-mail through the one mail account and show amounts in the install's default currency. Nothing binds a storefront around a listener.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** Nothing in the notification path. PR 53 (in PR 83) records the storefront with a payment and serves it again while the paid order is built, the only job-like path bound to a storefront; listeners are not wrapped. No open PR gives the order a storefront column: GCC-05 slice 2 (orders.storefront_country) is on a branch without a PR, CI not read.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) the order's storefront record read through one OrderNotificationContext (default storefront for legacy orders); (2) listeners run inside StorefrontContext::serve for their order and restore the previous context after each job; (3) profile, template, language and currency lookups (I1-01 to I1-03) take the order's storefront; (4) tests with two orders of different storefronts on one worker.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** none
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `none` No test builds a notification per storefront; the payment-callback tests bind a storefront only while the order is built
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Order storefront record (GCC-05 slice 2, no PR). Precondition: PR 53 builds a paid order inside its storefront (StorefrontPaymentCallbackTest, A1-13). Profiles and templates of I1-01 to I1-03; amounts in the order's currency need GCC-05.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [CODED] An order of a KW customer produces a push and an e-mail built from KW's profile, template, language and currency (no storefront on the order and no per-store lookup in the listeners // CODED on PR 164. Of the criterion's four words, LANGUAGE and CURRENCY are proved. Language for every recipient the senders name and for the two they deliberately skip: the buyer (push title in Arabic, not "Order"; e-mail template title in Arabic, not "Your order is placed"; and the same on an /api request, where getDefaultLanguage answers from the locale), the delivery man, the seller against a buyer who reads a DIFFERENT language, the vendor's own order-received e-mail, the till's buyer, and the promoter and admin who deliberately read the platform's. Currency for the push AND the e-mail body: deliveryman_charge moves from the platform conversion to the money the order holds, and every amount in the order-placed body likewise, rendered by the REAL mailer inside the wrap rather than under Mail::fake(), which leaves the view unrendered. NOT PROVED: "profile" and "template". A per-storefront mail/SMS/push profile is requirement I1-01 and exists in NO branch (MailConfigServiceProvider still replaces the whole mail config once at boot; there is no notification_channel_profiles table anywhere). Per-country notification templates are I1-03 and exist in no branch (email_templates, notification_messages and notification_template_mappings have no store column). This slice cannot meet criterion 1 however good it is, and does not claim to. Also not proved within "currency", both added by the review round on its own measurements: the amount is the order's money only while that market's currency row is ACTIVE (with the row switched off the push shows the platform conversion, which is the base layer's contract, now pinned), and the push carries no currency code or symbol beside the number, so the apps decide how to read it. Named tests: nine in tests/Feature/Storefront/OrderNotificationStorefrontTest.php, seven for language and two for currency; 32 tests / 108 assertions on SQLite and on MariaDB 10.11.14.

A reviewer who requires an out-of-process worker would want one more test.
