#### I1-07. Necessary notifications for old orders continue when a store stops selling

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 412 | GCC-12 (preserve prior orders, payments, refund, support, webhook processing); Discovery E06 section 2.7, Q-40 | reviews: legal, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** When a store stops selling (browse-only, not sellable, paused), the notifications an existing order needs still go out: status changes, refunds, due payments, payment confirmations and support replies. Only marketing and new-sale messages stop.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** No pause exists yet (GCC-12 is I2's). On main maintenance mode does not touch queues, the scheduler or the REST API (Discovery E06 2.7), and no listener checks a store's state, so order notifications are not gated. Nothing classifies a notification as necessary or marketing; the abandoned-cart job checks only its own switch.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 42 and 49 (in PR 83): the sale gate (storefront.sellable) is on the routes that start a sale only; cancellation, refund, due-payment and payment-status routes stay open, pinned by StorefrontSalesRoutesTest. The abandoned-cart job is not stopped for a store that cannot sell, so a reminder can link to a store that refuses checkout.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) classify every notification type as necessary or marketing in one registry, with a test that fails on an unclassified type; (2) a test per necessary type that its push and e-mail still go out for an order of a non-sellable storefront; (3) marketing and cart reminders skip such a storefront; (4) the GCC-12 pause (I2) must gate marketing only, never the listeners.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#42](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/42) `2534953`; [lavkoo#49](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/49) `b52d9b2`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`; [lavkoo#123](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/123) `53b1d22f`; [lavkoo#125](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/125) `6b1bf942`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` StorefrontSalesRoutesTest: every route that starts a new sale carries the sale gate; refunds, cancellations, callbacks and due payments of placed orders carry none (tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontSalesRoutesTest.php; PR 42 @2534953: Tests SQLite 8.2/8.3; PR 83 @8ff3410: also Storefront tests MySQL 8 and MariaDB 11.4.2)
  - `none` No test sends a notification for an order of a storefront that cannot sell
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** The GCC-12 pause is I2's (Q-40 open). The necessary-versus-marketing line needs a legal reading per country. Skipping reminders needs the sellable answer of StorefrontLegalReadiness (PR 38).
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] Cancel, refund, due-payment and payment-status routes of a placed order carry no sale gate (StorefrontSalesRoutesTest::test_refunds_cancellations_callbacks_and_obligations_on_placed_orders_stay_open; PR 42 @2534953 SQLite 8.2/8.3; PR 83 @8ff3410 also Storefront MySQL 8, MariaDB 11.4.2)
  - [CODED] Order status, refund and due-payment push and e-mail are sent for an order of a storefront that may not sell (PR 123 (draft, tests only, two files, 969 insertions, no source change; base gcc/p1-storefront-lifecycle at 7e19d284, which is PR 111's head, head 53b1d22f of gcc/p1-notification-stop-pins): StorefrontStopNotificationsTest, 18 behaviours over 10 data rows = 180 tests, 3768 assertions, with the support trait FakesPushNotifications (the stored project key, the operator's message texts, the device-token columns and the Google and Firebase hosts answered by a stub that records the wire message). CI run 368 (pull_request, 22 of 23 jobs green, only "Deployment overlay (artifact, deploys nothing)" red by design; all jobs ran the merge of 53b1d22f into 7e19d284, commit 3d86c1f): green on "Storefront tests, MySQL 8" (MySQL 8.0.46, PHP 8.3) and "Storefront tests, MariaDB 11.4.2", both running tests/Unit/Storefront and tests/Feature/Storefront, 1121 tests, 8428 assertions, nothing skipped — and those two directories list exactly 1121 tests at this head, 180 of them this class, so it ran inside both real-engine totals — and on "Tests, SQLite (PHP 8.3)" (whole suite, 7410 tests, 99 skipped, none of them in this class). The strict-mode MariaDB job does not run tests/Feature/Storefront, and under DB_STRICT_TESTS=1 every test of the class skips, so strict mode is no evidence for it. Driven over HTTP on an order the real API checkout placed before the state, in the 8 states in which a storefront may not sell (Kuwait browse only, maintenance, redirect; the default storefront browse only; back to draft; back to ready; active with its legal texts and their review withdrawn; active with its currency and its tax decision withdrawn) and in 2 controls that sell: every row asserts the lifecycle state, mode and the exact set of unmet StorefrontPreflight prerequisites, and each step asserts the row it changed plus exactly one push to each device it names, comparing type, order_id, message_key, notification body and refund_id. Told: the customer for each status the administrator gives (confirmed, processing, out for delivery, delivered; canceled) and for the statuses given from the vendor panel, the seller API v3 status and order-update routes, the seller API v2 and the delivery-man API; the customer for a rejected refund (administrator on a shop order and on a vendor order, vendor panel, seller API v3, seller API v2) and for a paid refund (administrator, shop order only); the vendor as well for the cancellation of its order, for a rejected refund in each of those routes but the seller API v2, and for a new refund request from the app and from the website (key refund_request_status_changed_by_admin; RefundListener's own request branch is unreachable); the delivery man for the cancellation; and the customer for the amount of an edited order returned. Every row ends with the new sale the shopper had waiting: 422 storefront_not_sellable with no order saved and the cart kept in the 8 states, accepted in the 2 controls. NOT proven, and false of the application today: no e-mail is sent for a status, a refund or a due payment (OrderStatusListener and RefundListener only push, OrderEditDuePaymentListener never calls sendingMail, and the only order mails are order placed and new order), so the test merely pins that nothing reaches the default mailer's array transport after every step — a tripwire, not proof of a send, and blind to a non-default mailer; and the due-payment push reaches no device, in a storefront that sells either, because order_edit_due_payment_message has no entry in PushNotificationTrait::pushNotificationMessage, so the test asserts the event is raised once for the vendor and that no push is sent, and no customer is told of a due payment at all. Also not covered: a push to a device a step does not name is not excluded; and the MyFatoorah refund, the approval of a refund and its payment by a vendor, the bulk status route, the delivery-man assignment, the order-edit notification and a queued listener run by a worker (the queue is sync here) are not driven.)
  - [CODED] Abandoned-cart reminders and other marketing sends skip a storefront that may not sell (PR 125 (6b1bf942): the abandoned-cart reminder and the admin WhatsApp cart-marketing list skip a cart whose products belong to a storefront that may not sell, and a skipped cart writes NO ledger row, so it is reminded once the storefront sells again. Four defects were found and fixed, two of them by the reviewers: a storefront switched off in configuration alone was still invited (fixed in StorefrontMarketingStop, deliberately not in the shared StorefrontSellingGate, because a gate refusing an unserved country would refuse money already taken); an N+1 of one SELECT per cart group on the admin page, now one query per 500 ids; a cart group naming two countries judged by its first row alone, which both missed a stopped storefront and over-reached into a selling one; and the WhatsApp cart total and the admin list summing money of storefronts trading in different currencies into one unmarked figure, so while the storefront-currency switch is on a customer whose carts name more than one storefront is left out of the list and refused by the send. Mutations run and killed, including the reviewers' own surviving one (countryOfCartGroup reading a group's last row rather than its first). Saudi default-off proved by a query count of ZERO reads while storefronts are off — not a setting, not a table, not a cart. CI run id 44517678226 (pull_request, head 6b1bf942), read job by job on 2026-11-19: 22 of 23 jobs success. Green on 'Storefront tests, MySQL 8' and 'Storefront tests, MariaDB 11.4.2', both 1118 tests / 6073 assertions; on the whole suite 'Tests, SQLite' 7407 tests / 798843 assertions on PHP 8.3 and on PHP 8.2; and on strict-mode MariaDB 11.4.2 (4021 / 35470), address (1045 / 4956 on both engines), fulfillment and security (1703 / 8897 MySQL, 8900 MariaDB), the ten sales-channel and cost shards, both PHP syntax jobs and the secret scan. The single red is 'Deployment overlay (artifact, deploys nothing)', red by design and not this branch's: its step refuses because firebase-messaging-sw.js, public/firebase-messaging-sw.js and robots.txt differ between the deploy baseline 698aa1e and the BASE branch gcc/p1-storefront-lifecycle, and this head adds none of those three files. NOT MET: only two of the four marketing types ask the stop. The admin push broadcast (one FCM topic plus every approved seller) and the referral push (SendReferralRegistrationNotificationListener) do not, so a customer of a stopped market still receives them; scoping the broadcast needs a topic per country and matching subscriptions in both Flutter apps, i.e. a release. One message per storefront, and storefront decimals on the figure, are also left undone.)
  - [MET] A registry lists every notification type as necessary or marketing, and a test fails for an unclassified type (PR 125 (draft, base gcc/p1-storefront-lifecycle at 7e19d284, head 6b1bf942, behind GCC_STOREFRONTS_ENABLED which ships off): NotificationTypeRegistry classifies every notification type the code sends as necessary or marketing — 68 push, 22 e-mail, 4 WhatsApp — and a scan test fails in BOTH directions: a type that is sent but not listed, and a listed type nothing sends. #### I1-08. Marketing sends respect consent

- **State: NOT STARTED** | plan lines 413 | DECISIONS (marketing and legal consents have correct scopes); Discovery E06-F09, Q-15; A2-13 | reviews: legal, native-arabic, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A marketing message (newsletter, abandoned-cart e-mail, WhatsApp template, push broadcast, SMS) is sent only to a customer whose consent for that store, channel and purpose is on record, and carries a working unsubscribe. Necessary notifications never depend on it. Recording the consent is A2-13; this is the sender side.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** No marketing consent record for any channel; the only consent is the analytics cookie (PrivacyGate). Baseline guards: the abandoned-cart job is off until enabled and skips guests unless a second switch is on. The reminder e-mail has no unsubscribe link, CartMarketingController sends WhatsApp templates to the customers an admin picks without a consent check, subscriptions holds an e-mail only, and the broadcast goes to the global topic.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** Nothing. PR 43's write-once legal acceptance recorder (82b6dfa) is the pattern to mirror for consent rows (A2-13); no sender in any tree reads a consent.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) the consent table and gate service (A2-13); (2) every marketing sender asks the gate: newsletter, abandoned-cart job, cart-marketing send, WhatsApp automation, push broadcast, SMS; (3) a signed unsubscribe link and route in every marketing e-mail; (4) WhatsApp opt-in evidence kept; (5) per-country rules from legal review; (6) tests with and without consent for each sender.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** none
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `none` No consent or unsubscribe test in main, PR 83 or PR 75
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** A2-13 supplies the records. Legal review of opt-in rules per country (Discovery Q-15, outside this audit). Arabic consent texts need native review. WhatsApp template rules at the provider.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [CODED] A marketing send is skipped for a customer with no consent for that store and channel (no consent record or gate in any tree // CODED on PR 163. A marketing send is skipped for a customer with no consent for that MARKET and channel, for the e-mail channel and the WhatsApp channel, including that consent for the wrong market, the wrong channel or the wrong purpose does not let a send through; the WhatsApp sender's dormancy is measured, not read. NOT PROVED: "that STORE" (storefront). The PUSH channel is not gated at all -- a topic broadcast has no per-customer recipient to ask about (tracker I1-05) -- and SMS has no marketing sender on main. Named tests: AbandonedCartConsentTest::test_a_customer_without_consent_is_not_reminded and CartMarketingConsentTest::test_a_customer_who_has_not_agreed_is_skipped_and_nothing_is_sent_to_them.

#### I1-09. Notification sends are idempotent and logged

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 414 | Discovery E06 sections 2.2 to 2.4 (E06-F16, E06-F18) | reviews: security, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Every notification attempt (push, e-mail, SMS, WhatsApp, in-app) is recorded once with channel, store, template key, an idempotency key, status and provider answer or error, without raw phone or e-mail. A replay of the same business event sends nothing twice, a failed send is retried within a budget, and an admin can read the log of their store.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Two ledgers exist for single features: abandoned_cart_reminders is written before the mail leaves and is unique on (cart_group_id, stage); seller_notification_deliveries has a unique digest key (per its own documentation). Mail listeners are queued with 2 tries, but sendingMail and OrderPlacedListener catch the send exception, so a failed SMTP send through them never reaches failed_jobs. Everything else is fire and forget: sendNotificationToHttp returns the answer or false and stores nothing; SMS, Zender and WhatsApp failures are only logged.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 55 (in PR 75): the first-order referral notification was added as one event where the sender reads a group of events, which raised an error after the order committed; it is now a group of one, so the order completes, empties the cart and notifies the referrer once. No PR adds a delivery log or an idempotency key for transactional notifications.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) a notification_deliveries ledger (channel, storefront, event key, recipient reference hash, unique idempotency key, status, provider id, error, attempts, times) written before sending and updated after; (2) one wrapper for the push, mail, SMS and WhatsApp senders; (3) retry rules and a visible final failure per store; (4) retention and PII redaction; (5) an admin list scoped by staff country; (6) tests with duplicate events and Http::fake.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#55](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/55) `cbd976a`; [lavkoo#75](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/75) `47b78ab`; [lavkoo#122](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/122) `ce8786b7`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` ReferredCustomersFirstOrderTest (2): the first order of a referred customer completes, empties the cart and marks the referral used; the referrer is notified once (OrderPlacedEvent, type promoter) (tests/Feature/Referral/ReferredCustomersFirstOrderTest.php; PR 55 @cbd976a and PR 75 @47b78ab: Tests SQLite 8.2/8.3 (no MySQL or MariaDB group covers tests/Feature/Referral))
  - `none` No test of the abandoned-cart ledger, of a duplicate order event, of a failed FCM, SMS or mail send, or of any delivery log
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Retention and redaction rules for logs are not decided. I1-01 to I1-03 supply profile, store and template fields; the order storefront record (GCC-05 slice 2); PushNotificationTrait and EmailTemplateTrait are shared files.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [CODED] The first order of a referred customer completes and notifies the referrer once (ReferredCustomersFirstOrderTest (2 tests); PR 55 @cbd976a and PR 75 @47b78ab: Tests SQLite 8.2/8.3)
  - [CODED] An abandoned-cart reminder for one cart group and stage is sent once, also after a run that died halfway (lavkoo PR 122 (draft, base main, head ce8786b7, 7 test files and one new CI group, no application file): tests/Feature/Retention/AbandonedCartReminderLedgerTest (5 tests, 29 assertions) drives cart:remind-abandoned with Mail::fake and with a mailer that throws. CI run 367 (pull_request, head ce8786b7): the new group "Notification and localization tests" on MySQL 8.0.46 and MariaDB 11.4.2 (37 tests, 174 assertions, success, no skips) and the whole suite on SQLite PHP 8.2 and 8.3 (6268 tests, 97 skipped, one failure, TorodShipmentDiscoveryTest, red on main); this class is in no skip or failure list on any of the four engines. Asserted: two runs send one AbandonedCartReminderMail to the cart's customer at stage 1 and leave one ledger row carrying the customer id, the address, the cart value and the item count; stage 2 of the same cart group is sent once and leaves a second row; a run whose mailer throws leaves its row and the next run sends nothing, so the pinned behaviour is AT MOST ONCE and not once — that reminder is never delivered and nothing retries it; a run that loses the insert to another worker sends nothing and still ends successfully. Audit mutations on that head: disabling the already-reminded guard of AbandonedCartService::findAbandoned reddens three of the five tests, deleting the ledger row when the send throws reddens the died-halfway test, and writing the row only after a successful send reddens the died-halfway and the lost-race tests, so the row-before-mail order is pinned. Not covered: a delivery after a run that died halfway (no test delivers a reminder after a failed run, and none kills the process rather than the send); the unique key on (cart_group_id, stage) of the live table, since no migration or SQL file in the repository creates abandoned_cart_reminders and the class builds the table itself with that key — removing the key from the test's own schema, with no application change, reddens only the key test and the lost-race test while both dedupe tests stay green, so what the tests prove is the PHP guard in findAbandoned and the live key stays unverified; sent_at is written before the attempt and no test reads it or the admin counters that count a not-null sent_at as sent, so a failed send being indistinguishable from a delivered one is read from the code, not pinned; and no test here pins an idempotency key or a delivery record for a duplicate order event or for any other channel (push, SMS, WhatsApp, transactional mail).)
  - [MISSING] A replay of the same business event gives one push and one e-mail per recipient (OrderManager dispatches order events with no idempotency key)
  - [MET] Every attempt is stored with channel, store, template key, status and provider error, without raw phone or e-mail (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `notification_send_attempts` (migration 2026_12_31_200000) and `App\Services\Notification\NotificationAttemptLog`, the only writer: channel, storefront_country, template_key, recipient_type, status, attempt, is_final and provider_error. No message and no address -- `recipient_digest` is a SHA-256 of the address so one recipient's attempts can be counted and read together without the table becoming a second copy of the customer list, and `withoutAddresses()` takes e-mail addresses and runs of digits long enough to be a telephone number or a device token out of the provider's error, because an SMTP or FCM error quotes the address it failed on. It writes nothing until its migration has run, and a failure to write never fails a send. Both senders go through it: `PushNotificationTrait::sendNotificationToHttp()` and `EmailTemplateTrait::writeAndSendMail()`, whose catch block was EMPTY. A DEFECT found while wiring it, and the reason nothing could ever have checked this: `sendNotificationToHttp()` is declared `bool|string|null`, so PHP coerced the FCM `Response` through `__toString()` into its body and the status code of a refusal never left the trait -- an HTTP 401 for a revoked service account arrived at every caller looking exactly like a success. `tests/Feature/Notifications/NotificationAttemptsAreStoredAndRetriedTest` (10) on MariaDB; teeth: storing nothing turns 7 red and errors 2, storing the raw address turns 1 red, dropping the scrubbing turns 1 red.)
  - [MET] A failed send is retried within a stated budget, and the final failure is visible to that store's admin (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `App\Services\Notification\SendsWithinABudget` runs the send up to `gcc.notifications.attempts` times, stores every attempt and marks only the last as final -- a failure a retry fixed is noise, a final one is an undelivered message. The budget SHIPS AT 1, which is exactly what the platform does today, and the first test asserts that: one attempt, one row, the same answer handed to the same caller; `gcc.notifications.retry_delay_ms` is 0 for the same reason, because a backoff that holds a web request open is a worse failure than the one it recovers from. What counts as a failure is the CHANNEL's judgement rather than an exception, since FCM answers 401 without throwing anything. Visible to that store's admin on the notification-accounts screen: `NotificationAccountBoard::row()` carries the count of that market's undelivered messages beside each channel and `undeliveredMessages()` lists them, narrowed by the employee's own countries -- an account can be configured, active and WRONG, and until now that read there exactly like a working one. Teeth: pinning the budget at 1 turns 2 red, marking every attempt final turns 2 red, ignoring the staff scope in the list turns 1 red; the refusal has its unrestricted twin.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** Test-first on a branch off main: tests/Feature/Notifications/NotificationAttemptTest with Http::fake and Mail::fake pinning today's silent failures. Then migration notification_deliveries, NotificationDeliveryLedger::attempt(key, channel, storefront) and a decorator used by sendNotificationToHttp and sendingMail behind GCC_NOTIFICATION_LOG (off). Tests: duplicate event sends once, a failure is recorded and retried, PII hashed.
- **Open owner decisions.** Retention period and redaction rule for delivery logs (not decided), and whether the log is per store or central.
- **Verifier note (not applied automatically).** exists_now and ac[1].e: States as fact that abandoned_cart_reminders is unique on (cart_group_id, stage). No migration or SQL in the repository defines that table or key (E06 section 2.11 lists it among tables without a tracked definition); the only source is the command's own comment, so it is UNVERIFIED against the live schema. The guard that is in code is AbandonedCartService::findAbandoned, which drops every cart group that already has a ledger row for the stage. Proposed: Two ledgers exist for single features: abandoned_cart_reminders is written before the mail leaves and findAbandoned skips groups that already have a row for the stage (the command's comment says the table is unique on (cart_group_id, stage), but no migration in the repository defines the table or the key: UNVERIFIED against the live schema); seller_notification_deliveries has a unique digest key (per its own docume
- **Update after the assessment.**  PR 122 (ce8786b7, run 367): the abandoned-cart reminder ledger is pinned (5 tests): the real behaviour is AT MOST ONCE and a failed send looks like a sent one (the row is written before the mail, the stats count it as sent); the key of the live table is in no file. Not audited: no state changes here.

#### I1-12. Coupon amounts in each store's currency; legacy conversions verified

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 424, 428 | GCC-17; DECISIONS (local pricing, no automatic FX); Discovery E02, E08, Q-04, Q-07, Q-08, Q-38 | reviews: finance, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A coupon's discount, minimum purchase and cap are entered, stored, shown and compared in the currency of its store (KWD, BHD and OMR with three decimals), never converted from Saudi amounts. Existing coupons keep their stored values and convert exactly as before while storefronts are off, proven by a round-trip test in both currency models.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** coupons has no currency or store column and its code is unique across the table. CouponService passes discount, min_purchase and max_discount through currencyConverter (system default currency to USD when the multi-currency model is on, unchanged otherwise); OrderManager compares them with the cart's stored amounts; pages show them with webCurrencyConverter at the current rate, so a stored value follows later rate changes. No test touches currencyConverter, CouponService or a converted coupon.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** Nothing for coupons: Coupon.php and CouponService.php are identical to main in the PR 83 tree. The GCC-05 branch gcc/p1-storefront-currency (no PR, CI not read) stores a non-default storefront's amounts as they are (rate one), so coupon amounts for such a storefront must skip the USD conversion in CouponService; that branch does not touch coupons.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) a store or currency column on coupons and a rule on whether one code may exist in two stores; (2) CouponService writes the store's unit and keeps the legacy conversion for the default storefront; (3) getTotalCouponAmount compares in the cart's unit; (4) admin, vendor and coupon pages show the currency and decimals; (5) a round-trip test matrix (single and multi-currency model, KWD, BHD, OMR); (6) legacy rows untouched.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#87](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/87) `44b53616`; [lavkoo#96](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/96) `e624e43e`; [lavkoo#120](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/120) `2ab62bb5`; [lavkoo#129](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/129) `7ca87a9f`

#### I1-13. Coupons name their eligible products and store

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 424 | GCC-17 (each store has its own eligible items and redemption); Discovery Q-04, R-054 | reviews: owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A coupon names what it applies to (products, categories, brands or sellers) per store, and only those lines count towards its eligible subtotal and discount. A coupon of one store is refused in another store's cart, and the coupon lists show a customer only coupons that can apply in their storefront.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Eligibility is by seller only: coupons.seller_id (NULL in-house, 0 every seller, n one seller), coupon_bearer and customer_id (0 or one customer). getTotalCouponAmount sums the cart lines of the matching seller; Coupon has no product, category, brand or store field. The coupon list, applicable_list and seller-wise endpoints list every active coupon.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 48 (in PR 83): Cart carries StorefrontCartScope and getCartListQueryAPI, which coupon application reads, reads through Cart, so with storefronts on the eligible subtotal is made from the request storefront's rows. The GCC-14c note says a promotion has no country yet (GCC-17). Customer app PR 7 drops the applied coupon and the coupon lists on a storefront change. No coupon table, rule or list changed.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) coupon rules for products, categories and brands (additive table or JSON) evaluated in getTotalCouponAmount, getGroupWiseCouponArray and the POS coupon path; (2) a store on the coupon and a refusal of another store's coupon; (3) coupon list, applicable_list and seller-wise lists narrowed by the storefront (B-16); (4) the eligible amount shown to the customer; (5) tests for every rule combination and flag-off identity.

#### I1-14. Per-store coupon limits and budget

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 424 | GCC-17 (limits, redemption); Discovery R-054, Q-04 | reviews: finance, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A coupon's usage rules are per store: the per-customer limit and the meaning of first order counted in the store (or per identity, by decision), a total redemption cap and a spending budget in the store's currency. Redemption is atomic, so two simultaneous checkouts cannot exceed the cap or the budget, and an exhausted coupon is refused with its own message.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Per-customer limit only: Coupon.limit is compared with the order groups this customer already placed with the code (OrderManager::getTotalCouponAmount; the applicable-coupon list counts the customer's orders for the code), over every order whatever the store. first_order coupons are refused once the customer has any order. Dates and status apply. No total cap, budget, spend field or per-store count exists, and redemption uses no lock or counter table.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** Nothing. The GCC-14c design note records that a coupon's use count must not be narrowed by an employee's country, or a limit would be bypassed by an order in another country.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) the decision on limit and first order per store or per identity (Q-04); (2) a coupon_redemptions ledger (coupon, order group, storefront, amount, currency) unique per order group, used for limits, caps and budget; (3) coupon fields for cap and budget (amount, period) and a spend read-out for the admin; (4) refusal messages in Arabic and English; (5) a concurrency test on MySQL and MariaDB.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#120](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/120) `2ab62bb5`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `none` No test of the limit, the first-order rule or any cap in main, PR 83 or PR 75
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Q-04 and the budget meaning of GCC-17 are open. Currency units are I1-12's. The concurrency harness (tests/Feature/Concurrency: ConcurrencyHarnessTest and the stock, MyFatoorah, Noon order, Odoo posting, order ingestion, package split, return decision and attention suites) is in main and runs in the MySQL 8 and MariaDB 11.4.2 'concurrency' jobs and can be reused; wallet concurrency tests exist only on gcc/p0-wallet-concurrency-lock, not in main, PR 83 or PR 75.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] A customer cannot redeem a coupon more often than its limit (Pull request 120 (draft, base gcc/p1-storefront-six-country-isolation at e624e43e, the head of pull request 96 and the branch's merge base, head 2ab62bb5, four files, 1,234 lines added and none removed, TESTS ONLY, no application file) adds tests/Feature/Storefront/CouponBehaviourPinsTest (25 cases) with the support trait Tests\Support\BuildsCouponCheckoutWorld; four of the cases pin the limit through the real apply route (/api/v1/coupon/apply) and the real checkout (/api/v1/customer/order/place): a coupon of limit 2 is applied and checked out twice and refused the third time with 202 and the answer 'sorry this coupon is not valid for this user!', after which the checkout places the third order with discount 0 and coupon code 0 while another customer still applies it; only the customer's orders that carry that coupon count, so an order without a coupon and an order with another coupon do not, and that other coupon still applies; a canceled order still counts against the limit of its coupon; and the count is per order group, so a checkout of two vendors writes two order rows under one order_group_id and is one redemption. CI run 364 (pull_request, merge commit 198560f of 2ab62bb5 into e624e43e, a fast-forward, so the tree tested is the head's): 'Storefront tests, MySQL 8' (8.0.46) and 'Storefront tests, MariaDB 11.4.2' each ran tests/Unit/Storefront and tests/Feature/Storefront, 926 tests, 4,319 assertions, nothing skipped and nothing failed, and 926 is exactly what phpunit --list-tests gives for those two directories at the head, this class's 25 cases included; 'Tests, SQLite (PHP 8.3)' ran the whole suite, 7,215 tests, 99 skipped with no coupon case among them, no failure; the deployment overlay job is red by design. Mutations on a throwaway copy of the head, reverted: an off-by-one in the limit comparison of OrderManager::getTotalCouponAmount (>= for >) fails all four cases; counting order rows instead of order groups (the groupBy dropped from the use count) fails the order-group case; writing the requested code onto the order instead of the per-group coupon answer fails the two cases that read the placed order; and the branch's recorded mutation, dropping the coupon scope from the use count, fails the scoped case. Not covered: the limit counted per store (I1-14.2), a total cap or a budget (I1-14.3 and I1-14.4), two concurrent checkouts, the applicable-coupon list, which no case reads, and the limit comparison in OrderManager::getGroupWiseCouponArray, where the same off-by-one leaves all 25 cases green because that function returns early with coupon code 0 whenever the discount handed to it is 0; and no case has run on a strict server, since the strict-mode MariaDB job runs only StrictModeTest, SalesChannels, OperationalCosts and the two Fulfillment directories, and under DB_STRICT_TESTS=1 the trait skips every case that calls the apply route or the checkout until PR 89 adds OrderManager::countCouponUses.)
  - [MET] A first_order coupon is refused once the customer has an order (Pull request 120 (draft, base gcc/p1-storefront-six-country-isolation at e624e43e, head 2ab62bb5, TESTS ONLY: four test files, no application file) adds tests/Feature/Storefront/CouponBehaviourPinsTest (25 cases), three of which pin the first-order rule of OrderManager::getTotalCouponAmount through the real customer API: a customer with no order has the coupon applied (the apply route /api/v1/coupon/apply answers coupon_type first_order and discount 5, and the checkout /api/v1/customer/order/place places the order with coupon_code FIRST, discount 5 and the total lowered by 5); once that customer has an order the apply route answers 202 with the sorry_this_coupon_is_not_valid_for_this_user message (the key and the branch, not the wording: the expected string is built from the same translate call as the code) and the same checkout call places the next order with discount 0 and coupon_code 0; and an earlier order set to canceled still refuses it, while a newcomer with no order still has it applied. The coupon is a row inserted with limit 0, the value the admin's own form stores for this type and one the code never compares for it. Three mutations on a throwaway copy of the head: never refusing on an earlier order fails both refusal cases; ignoring canceled orders fails the canceled case only; counting every customer's orders instead of this customer's fails the newcomer case. Not covered: first order counted per store rather than over every order of the customer (I1-14.2, open on Q-04), the total cap and the budget (I1-14.3, I1-14.4), any surface other than the customer API apply and checkout routes (the web coupon form, which calls the same method, and the POS have no case), and no run on a strict server — the strict-mode MariaDB job runs only StrictModeTest, SalesChannels, OperationalCosts, Unit/Fulfillment and Feature/Fulfillment, and under DB_STRICT_TESTS=1 every case that calls the apply route or the checkout skips until PR 89.)

#### I1-15. Campaigns: central ones choose stores, local ones belong to one store

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 422, 426 | GCC-17; Discovery E06 section 2.5 and E03 on experience_campaigns; B-16 | reviews: owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A central campaign (window, coupon rules, banners and page overlay, optional push) selects one, several or all markets, and each selected market runs its own local rules. A manager restricted to one country can create and run a local campaign for that country only. Campaign state, caches and statistics are kept per store.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Three kinds of 'campaign', none with a market. ExperienceCampaign is a scheduled page overlay (name, status, page, priority, window, overrides) cached as commerce_campaign_overrides_<page> with no storefront. Analytics campaigns are tracked short links with QR codes. Flash and featured deals, deal of the day and clearance sales are promotions with one active flash deal system-wide. No central campaign with per-store rules exists.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 82 (in PR 83): the analytics campaign screens (storeCampaign, toggleCampaign, campaignQr) are unavailable to an employee restricted to a country, tested on all seven analytics routes. PR 50: caches built from a storefront's products, flash deals among them, are kept per storefront. No campaign, deal or coupon table or screen gained a market; GCC-14c slice 4 (promotions) is not started.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) per-market coupon rules linked to a campaign (I1-12 to I1-14) and banners; (2) per-store reporting; (3) the GCC-17 owner decisions. DONE 2026-11-25: the market selection itself (`App\Services\Commerce\CampaignMarkets`, through the existing `storefront_entity_visibility` mapping, so no campaign table and no migration were needed), the local-campaign scope (a restricted manager creates, sees and changes campaigns of their own markets only), the per-storefront overlay cache key (which was already built and unmarked), and the conflict check narrowed to the markets a campaign actually runs in.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#50](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/50) `98191ff`; [lavkoo#82](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/82) `d1fc5f4`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` StaffTaxAndAnalyticsTest: every analytics screen (7 routes, campaign tools included) answers 404 to an employee restricted to KW and is unchanged for one with no country (tests/Feature/StaffScope/StaffTaxAndAnalyticsTest.php; PR 82 @d1fc5f4: Tests SQLite 8.2/8.3; PR 83 @8ff3410: Staff scope tests MySQL 8 and MariaDB 11.4.2)
  - `MariaDB 10.11.14` CampaignMarketTest: a campaign selects its markets and only those storefronts are served it; a restricted manager is offered their own markets, cannot widen by the form, cannot see or change another market's campaign, and two markets' campaigns no longer contest a slot (tests/Feature/Marketing/CampaignMarketTest.php; 16 green on this branch 2026-11-25, with four teeth measured)
  - `MariaDB 10.11.14` StorefrontCatalogueVisibilityCacheTest: the overlay cache is keyed per storefront and a campaign change forgets every storefront's copy (tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontCatalogueVisibilityCacheTest.php; 13 green)

- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** GCC-17 sets the principle (DECISIONS.md) but not the local-campaign, budget and flash-deal details. Local campaigns need GCC-14c slice 4 (unit G, G-09). The overlay cache key is B-16's remaining item (2). Coupon rules of I1-12 to I1-14 come first.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] A central campaign can select one, several or all markets and runs only in the selected ones (BUILT, 2026-11-25, and the engine was already there: `ExperienceCampaign` carries `HasStorefrontVisibility`, so a campaign with a mapping row is served only in the markets its rows name and one with none is served everywhere -- the `assigned` mode of `StorefrontVisibility`. **No screen could write such a row**, so every campaign anybody had created was platform-wide and this criterion described an empty table. `App\Services\Commerce\CampaignMarkets` + `CampaignController` now write it through the one mapping writer, and the Blade carries a multiple select beside the page and priority. `CampaignMarketTest::test_only_the_chosen_markets_storefront_is_served_the_campaign` walks the whole path: a campaign named for Kuwait dresses the Kuwaiti storefront's page and not the Emirati one, while an unmarked one dresses both. The same pass fixed a false refusal the markets exposed -- `CampaignRules::conflictsFor()` treated the Ramadan hero of Kuwait and the Ramadan hero of the Emirates as a §38 tie although no page ever carries both. 16 green on MariaDB 10.11.14; teeth: dropping the writer's `apply()` turns 8 red, dropping `marketsOverlap()` refuses the Emirati campaign again.)
  - [MISSING] Each selected market has its own coupon amounts, currency, limits, eligible products and budget (see I1-12 to I1-14; no campaign-to-rules link exists)
  - [MET] A manager restricted to KW creates and runs a local campaign for KW only and cannot see or change another market's (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `CampaignMarkets::listedFor()` shows a restricted manager the campaigns that run in a market of theirs; `mayChange()` refuses them a campaign that names a market outside their scope AND a platform-wide one, because changing a campaign that runs everywhere changes markets that are not theirs and silently confining somebody else's campaign to Kuwait is a bigger surprise than being told no. The line is drawn deliberately on the READ side too: a platform-wide campaign IS listed for them, because it dresses their own storefront and hiding it would hide something that is running in their shop. `CampaignMarketTest`: the Kuwaiti manager is not shown the Emirati campaign, cannot rename it and cannot delete it through the screen; deleting their own takes its mapping with it, so the next campaign to take that id does not inherit somebody else's markets. Teeth: dropping the `mayChange()` gate lets the rename and the delete through, 2 red.)
  - [MET] The experience-campaign overlay is cached per storefront (ALREADY BUILT AND NEVER MARKED -- verified on this branch 2026-11-25 rather than taken on trust. `app/Services/Commerce/CampaignResolver.php:49` keys the overlay through `StorefrontCatalog::cacheKey()`, and `:65` forgets EVERY storefront's copy plus the one a request belonging to none wrote, so a campaign that opens or closes reaches every market at once. Proved by two tests in `StorefrontCatalogueVisibilityCacheTest` (tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontCatalogueVisibilityCacheTest.php): `test_the_campaign_overlay_cache_is_not_served_across_storefronts` and `test_a_campaign_change_forgets_every_storefronts_overlay`, with `test_the_saudi_storefront_keeps_the_keys_it_writes_under_today` pinning `commerce_campaign_overrides_home` as the Saudi key so the default storefront's cache entries are the ones it has always written. 13 green on MariaDB 10.11.14. Both halves have teeth, measured: taking `StorefrontCatalog::cacheKey()` off the key makes Kuwait read the overlay Saudi Arabia warmed (1 red), and forgetting only the bare key leaves the Kuwaiti copy behind (1 red).)
  - [MET] The analytics campaign screens are unavailable to a country-restricted employee (StaffTaxAndAnalyticsTest::test_every_analytics_screen_is_unavailable_to_a_restricted_employee; PR 82 @d1fc5f4 SQLite; PR 83 @8ff3410 Staff scope MySQL 8, MariaDB 11.4.2)
- **(10) Next coding task.** The cache key and the market selection are done. Next, and both blocked on the owner rather than on code: GCC-17's per-market coupon amounts, limits and budget (I1-12 to I1-14, which also wait on the minor-unit decision B-16 records), and per-market campaign reporting, which needs the analytics rollup to gain a market dimension at all -- it has none today, so every reach figure a campaign screen prints is a platform total (coverage-gaps).
- **Open owner decisions.** GCC-17 (DECISIONS.md) decides that a central campaign may select markets and that each store has its own amounts, currency, limits, eligible items and redemption. Not written down: who may create and run a local campaign (plan line 426), what a campaign budget caps and who may raise it, and whether the one active flash deal stays system-wide or becomes per store (B-16).

#### I1-16. Languages per market

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 409; owner axis word 'languages' | DECISIONS (one customer identity; GCC-09 web and app customisation); owner axis word 'languages' | reviews: native-arabic, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Each market declares the languages it offers (at least Arabic and English), its default and direction. The website, the API lang header, the apps and notifications choose the language from that list; a code the market does not list is refused or falls back to the market default; switching market keeps the customer's language when the new market lists it.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** One language list for the whole install (business setting 'language': code, name, direction, status, default). The website keeps the choice in the session (SharedController); APILocalizationMiddleware reads the lang header (sa to ar, us to en, matches the store's own code such as 'sy'); customers, sellers and delivery men hold app_language (default 'en'). The customer app ships Arabic and English, picks language and country separately and maps Arabic to 'sy'. Nothing ties a language to a market.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 63 (in PR 75): the website switch refuses a code the store does not list (422); the API answers in the store's default (or first) language for an unlisted header and resolves en-US and ar-SA. PR 51: language choice by request area, not a substring test. PR 38 (in PR 83): config/gcc.php lists required_locales ['ar','en'] with locales_by_country, used only by legal readiness (I2). No PR gives a storefront its own list.
- **(5) What remains.** (1) and (4) are built and tested (2026-11-25): the list, the default and the direction per market, in `gcc.storefronts.language.lists_by_country` and in the storefront list API, and both notification senders write within the market's list. What is left: (2) the WEBSITE's own language switch and the API header are still narrowed to the SHOP's list and not to the market's, so a visitor can choose a language their market does not present on the website; (3) the language rule on a market switch, which is the customer app's half (axis J); (6) the Arabic code (the live store spells it `sa`, the apps use `ar`; production UNVERIFIED).

  - [MET] The storefront language switch refuses a code the store does not list and keeps the session language (LanguageSelectionTest::test_the_switch_refuses_a_code_the_store_does_not_list_and_keeps_the_language_of_the_session; PR 63 @de0a7d5: SQLite, Fulfillment + security MySQL 8, MariaDB 11.4.2)

  - [MET] Each storefront has its own language list, default language and direction (BUILT, 2026-11-25. `gcc.storefronts.language.lists_by_country` holds the languages each market PRESENTS, in the order an operator listed them, beside the `by_country` default that was already there. `App\Services\Storefront\MarketLanguage` answers all three questions in one place: `listOf()` (the market's list, dropping a locale the platform has not installed and a repeat), `of()` (the default, and a market that names a LIST but no default writes in the FIRST of its list -- a list with no default is an operator saying which languages a market presents, not which comes first, and the first is the only answer that needs no second decision), and `directionOf()`. **The direction is the one the platform's own language list DECLARES for that locale and is never derived from the language code**, which is the rule the website already follows and which `WebsiteDirectionTest` fails on -- so no second source of truth for direction was created. Each storefront of `GET api/v1/storefronts` now carries `languages`, `default_language` and `direction`, so an app can offer a market's own languages; **a market with no list of its own is listed with the PLATFORM's languages and not with an empty list**, because an app that read an empty list would offer no language at all and "this market has not been given a list yet" means "it presents what the shop presents". The lists ship EMPTY, so every market presents what the shop presents and the Saudi platform is unchanged -- the first test asserts exactly that. `tests/Feature/Storefront/MarketLanguageTest` (14 tests, 35 assertions) and `tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontListApiTest` (10 tests, 45 assertions; its exact-payload pin was updated on purpose, which is what that pin is for). Teeth: dropping the platform fallback from the listing turns 1 red. NOT covered: the website's own language switch is not yet narrowed to the market's list -- `SharedController::changeLanguage` still refuses only a code the SHOP does not list -- so a visitor can still choose a language their market does not present on the website, which is the remaining half and is recorded here rather than claimed.)
  - [MISSING] Switching market keeps the customer's language when the new market lists it and falls back to that market's default otherwise (**THE SERVER HALF IS BUILT AND TESTED, 2026-11-25, and the record is corrected rather than the state**: the criterion names what the customer APP does with its own stored choice, which is `fullstackfull/lavkooapp` and not this repository, so it stays MISSING. What was fixed here is a real defect of the server, reported by an earlier reviewer and unfixed until now: **the market's language list existed and NEITHER VISITOR PATH READ IT.** `SharedController::changeLanguage()` and `APILocalizationMiddleware` both checked the requested code against THE INSTALLATION'S languages -- the `business_settings` list -- so a visitor on a storefront whose operator had listed Arabic only could switch it to English and read an English page from a market that does not offer English, and an app could ask for the same in a header and be answered in it. Everything that writes TO a person already asked the market (`MessageLanguage`, `OrderNotificationContext`); what a person could ask the shop FOR did not. The rule now lives in one place, `LocalisedSettings::localeOfferedToVisitor()`, because two call sites with one rule between them is how the two halves came to disagree. The two paths answer differently ON PURPOSE: the website REFUSES, because a switch is a deliberate act and silently giving somebody a language they did not choose is worse than telling them no; the API FALLS BACK to the market's own language, because an installed app sends a header on every request and must be served a page whatever it asks for -- which is precisely this criterion's "falls back to that market's default otherwise", server-side. `MarketPresentsItsLanguagesToVisitorsTest` (tests/Feature/Localization/MarketPresentsItsLanguagesToVisitorsTest.php): 7 green on MariaDB 10.11.14, beside the 60 of the pre-existing `LanguageSelectionTest` which are unchanged. Teeth: removing the market check from the website turns 1 red, from the API 1 red. First test is the switch: a market that names no list is the shop, which is every market today and every market while storefronts are off. WHAT REMAINS is the app's own half -- its stored language choice and its local strings.)
  - [MET] A push or e-mail is rendered in the recipient's language when the recipient's market lists it, else in the market default (BUILT, 2026-11-25, on the I1-03 engine: the recipient's own `app_language` has always decided this, and what had no answer was a recipient whose chosen language the MARKET DOES NOT PRESENT. Writing to a customer in a language that market's storefront is not translated into sends them a message whose links do not lead where it reads, which is worse than writing in the market's own language -- so such a recipient is now written to in the market's language (`MarketLanguage::of()`), and a market that lists nothing presents whatever the platform presents, which is every market today. **Both senders were changed, because they are two code paths and a test of one proves nothing about the other:** `OrderNotificationContext::languageOf()` for a message about an order, where the market is the ORDER's own, and `MessageLanguage::of()` for every other message (a registration, a password reset, a wallet top-up), where it is the market in force -- so nothing had to be threaded through a sender to get the market. `MarketLanguageTest`: a Kuwaiti order's rider who READS English is written to in Arabic because the Kuwaiti storefront presents Arabic only, read off the title of the push that would have left the server; a rider whose language the market DOES present keeps it; and the same pair through the non-order sender, asserted on `getDefaultLanguage()` inside the callback. Teeth: removing the two guards turns exactly those two tests red -- one per sender, which is the check that both were wired. Neighbours green: OrderNotificationStorefrontTest 26, PushBodyLanguageTest 14, EmailTemplateBrandTest 9, LanguageSelectionTest 60, WebsiteDirectionTest 6, StorefrontMarketingStopTest 51. NOT covered: a message to a LIST of addresses still stays in the sender's own language, as before, because it has no single recipient whose market could be read.)
  - [CODED] Arabic shows right-to-left and English left-to-right on the website and in the customer app (Website half — lavkoo PR 122 (draft, base main, head ce8786b7, 7 test files and one new CI group, no application file): tests/Feature/Localization/WebsiteDirectionTest, 6 tests. CI run 367 (pull_request, head ce8786b7): the new group 'Notification and localization tests' on MySQL 8 and on MariaDB 11.4.2 (37 tests, 174 assertions, success, no skips; tests/Feature/Localization holds this class alone, 6 of those 37) and the whole suite on SQLite 8.2 and 8.3 (6268 tests, 792615 assertions, 97 skipped, one failure, TorodShipmentDiscoveryTest, red on main; this class neither failed nor skipped). Five behaviour tests assert that POST /change-language and, with no choice made, the default entry of the language list put that entry's direction in the session, and that the maintenance page, fetched through its real route /maintenance-mode and rendered from its real template, opens with an <html dir> equal to it: rtl for the Arabic entry (code 'sa', as this store's list spells it) and ltr for 'en', the opposite when the list says the opposite (so the direction is the list entry's and is not read off the language code), and ltr for an entry that names no direction. One structural test asserts that the templates of the one shipped theme that open a page are exactly the five it names and that each writes dir from the session value. Audit mutations on that head, each killing named tests: the direction derived from the language code in SharedController::changeLanguage turns 2 red, a fixed dir="ltr" in maintenance-mode.blade.php turns 4 red, and getDefaultLanguage() ignoring the entry's direction turns 1 red. App half — lavkooapp PR 19 (draft, base gcc/p1-customer-wallet-reload, head 0ed2c844, 6 test files and CI steps, no lib/ file): test/localization_direction_test.dart, 10 tests, app CI run 33 (push, head 0ed2c844, conclusion success), job storefront-header, step 'I1-16.5 Arabic reads right to left and English left to right in the app', 10 passed and none skipped in one default build (no --dart-define); job analyze-whole-project success (255 findings, the recorded number). Six tests assert behaviour: LocalizationController.isLtr is false for Arabic and true for English both when a saved language is restored and when one is chosen (4 tests; the choosing tests stop at the null HTTP client after the locale and the flag are set), a MaterialApp built inside the test with the controller's locale and Flutter's three global delegates reads right to left in Arabic and left to right in English (1 test), and the language list is exactly Arabic and English (1 test); the remaining 4 are source scans of lib/main.dart (the controller's locale inside a Consumer of the controller, the widgets delegate and supportedLocales built from AppConstants.languages, exactly one Directionality and it before the router, no locale resolution callback and no Localizations.override). Not covered: on the website only the maintenance page is proven to render — the shop, auction, blog and invoice layouts are scan-only; maintenance-mode.blade.php writes dir="{{ session('direction') }}" with no fallback, and an audit probe on that head (Arabic the default, a session holding no direction) rendered it as dir="", the session being filled only while the page body ran, a case no test exercises (PR 122 says a real request's boot masks it, which no test shows), and invoice.blade.php reads the session before calling getDefaultLanguage() in the same file; in the app the real MaterialApp.router and every routed page are never built, the splash the scan pins wraps itself in a fixed left-to-right Directionality, 16 widget files force TextDirection.ltr on parts of screens (8 calling Directionality directly, 8 through CustomDirectionalityWidget), and nothing ran on a device; AppConstants.languages carries no direction at all (isLtr is languageCode != 'ar'), so a direction taken from the market's language list is true of the website and not of the app.)

The strict-mode MariaDB job does not run tests/Feature/Storefront and is not proof for these tests.)
  - [MISSING] The Saudi baseline pages exist as SA version 1 with their dates
  - [MET] Retention and deletion rules for texts and for acceptance rows are defined and tested (BUILT, 2026-11-25. Both tables were created without a word about retention, which for a consent record is the first question a regulator asks, and `undefined` is the worst answer because every installation then decides by accident. `App\Services\Retention\LegalRecordRetention` is the definition, with the five rules in `rules()` so the service's docblock, the console command and the test cannot drift apart: (1) a text that was ever APPROVED is kept for good -- published, archived or revoked makes no difference, because an acceptance points at it by id, version and hash and the text in force on a date must stay showable; (2) revocation ENDS a version and does not remove one; (3) a DRAFT never approved may be removed past `gcc.retention.legal.draft_days`, which is the only removal these rules permit and ships at 0, meaning never -- asserted first; (4) an acceptance row is never deleted and never changed, which the model already enforces and these rules do not ask an exception to; (5) the identity is NOT on the acceptance -- it carries a `customer_id` or a `guest_hash` and no name, address or telephone number, so closing the account is what removes the person while the acceptance keeps the version and the hash, which is the retention answer for a consent record: keep what was agreed, lose who agreed it. The row that was approved and then put BACK to draft is the case the rule turns on, and the history wins over the status: `removableDrafts()` reads `approved_at` and `published_at`, not only `status`. `php artisan gcc:legal:retention` prints the rules and what they would remove and changes nothing; `--apply` removes the drafts it listed. `tests/Feature/Retention/LegalRecordRetentionTest` (8) on MariaDB; teeth: ignoring the budget turns 1 red, reading only the status turns 1 red, dropping a rule from the stated policy turns 2 red, making `plan()` apply turns 3 red.)

#### I2-05. Acceptance receipts: proof of the legal text a customer accepted, tied to the order

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 399 | GCC-11; E06 section 2.6 (no accepted-version record); PR 43 body | reviews: legal, security
- **(2) Functional requirement.** When a legal text needs explicit acceptance, the order keeps proof of the exact text the customer saw: storefront, legal entity, document type, locale, text id, version, hash, time and order, with identity kept only as far as the privacy policy allows.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** Only a client-side agreement checkbox with links to the global pages (_order-summary.blade.php). Nothing is stored with the order or the account (E06 section 2.6).
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 43 (82b6dfa, test-only): table storefront_legal_acceptances, model StorefrontLegalAcceptance, StorefrontLegalAcceptanceRecorder::recordIfRequired(), StorefrontLegalReadiness::currentTextFor(); gcc.storefronts.legal.explicit_acceptance (empty by default), store_identity (off), legal_entity (GCC_LEGAL_ENTITY). 8 tests. Nothing calls the recorder: no checkout writes a receipt.
- **(5) What remains.** 1) ~~Call the recorder from the web and API checkout inside the order transaction, with the order id~~ -- DONE: one call inside `generateOrder`'s transaction, covering all seven order-creating paths. 2) Privacy and legal review before it runs (PR 43 body). 3) A per-storefront legal entity (one env value today). 4) Retention and deletion rules. 5) Write-once is enforced by the model, which refuses an update and a delete, but there is no unique key on (order_id, document_type), so write-once does not by itself stop a SECOND caller writing a duplicate row -- which is why exactly one caller is now a test rather than a convention. 6) A defined outcome when no text exists at checkout: the recorder still throws, and the caller now swallows that and reports it, deliberately, because the order and possibly its money are already written in the same transaction. The swallow is NARROW: a concurrency error is RETHROWN (`DetectsConcurrencyErrors`), because `generateOrder` runs `DB::transaction(attempts: 3)` and swallowing a deadlock there would leave the order's transaction running after the engine had rolled its statement back -- a half-written order committing. 7) A guest checkout driven end to end, for the `is_guest` identity mapping.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#43](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/43) `82b6dfa`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `none` StorefrontLegalAcceptanceTest, 8 tests (recorded fields, not-required types, identity off, guest hash, later version, no text, legal entity) (refuted by the verifier: 'recorded fields' covers storefront, type, locale, version and hash (legal entity in its own test). legal_document_id, accepted_at and order_id are asserted by )
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Needs I2-04 (the customer must see the text that is recorded), the order legal-entity snapshot (entities and finance unit) and the privacy and legal review. The checkout files are also touched by the security set (CartManager, module-helper).
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [CODED] An explicit acceptance records storefront, legal entity, type, locale, text id, version, hash and time, and the order id when given (test_an_explicit_acceptance_records_the_current_text_version_and_its_hash; PR 83)
  - [MET] Nothing is recorded for a type that does not need explicit acceptance, and nothing without an effective text (test_nothing_is_recorded_for_a_type_that_does_not_need_explicit_acceptance, test_nothing_is_accepted_when_no_effective_text_exists; PR 83)
  - [MET] Identity is not kept by default; a guest is kept only as a SHA-256 hash when identity is allowed (test_identity_is_not_kept_unless_the_privacy_policy_allows_it, test_when_identity_is_allowed_a_guest_is_kept_only_as_a_hash; PR 83)
  - [MET] A recorded row keeps its version and hash when a newer text supersedes it (test_an_acceptance_keeps_its_text_when_a_later_version_supersedes_it; PR 83)
  - [MET] Every web and API order that needed acceptance has a receipt row carrying its order id (the receipt is written inside `OrderManager::generateOrder`'s own transaction, which is the ONE line every order of this platform is written at: three web checkouts, three `/api/v1` endpoints and the point-of-sale helper, seven call sites of `generateOrder` in all. Before this it was called from the three v1 endpoints the customer app posts to and from nowhere else, so the whole website placed orders that needed an accepted text and recorded nothing -- and the gap was invisible because the three covered paths were the three with tests. CheckoutLegalAcceptanceTest 11 to 15: `test_the_website_writes_a_receipt_and_not_only_the_customer_app` posts a real `POST /checkout-complete` with a real cart, a real saved address and the real middleware stack and reads the row back; `test_the_website_records_nothing_while_no_type_requires_acceptance` holds the shipped dormancy on that route too; `test_one_order_and_one_type_write_exactly_one_receipt`; `test_a_checkout_that_places_no_order_leaves_no_receipt`. 15 green, 71 assertions, SQLite. Teeth, run in this container: taking the call out of `generateOrder` fails 8 of the 15 and errors a 9th, the web test among them; putting ONE of the three outer calls back fails the duplicate test with 'Failed asserting that 2 is identical to 1', which is why the outer calls were REMOVED rather than left as belt and braces -- the table has no unique key and a receipt is written once and never updated, so two rows saying the same thing would be a corrupted record of consent. The lexical position is pinned separately by tests/Unit/Architecture/LegalReceiptHasOneWriterTest, 3 tests: exactly one file under `app/` asks for a receipt, the call is inside the still-open `DB::transaction(` that writes the order, and the seven order-creating call sites are counted so an eighth has to be looked at by somebody; teeth, removing the call fails the transaction test. NOT COVERED, said out loud: a refusal raised AFTER the order row is inserted -- the stock race -- cannot be forced from a test, so what is proven about rollback is that a checkout which places no order leaves no receipt, not that a mid-transaction refusal unwinds one; and no test drives a GUEST checkout, so the `is_guest` mapping that keeps a guest id out of the `customer_id` column is reasoned and not measured. Identity ships off, so that mapping is dormant today.)
  - [MET] Receipts have retention and deletion rules, and the legal entity is per storefront (**RAISED MISSING -> MET, 2026-11-26.** Both clauses were built by other tracks and this card was never re-read. RETENTION AND DELETION: `LegalRecordRetentionTest` (8 cases) -- with no budget chosen nothing is removable, a text that was ever approved is kept for good whatever the budget says, a never-approved draft past the budget is removed while one inside it is kept, an acceptance row cannot be deleted or changed by these rules OR BY ANYTHING ELSE, the acceptance holds no identity of its own so closing the account removes the person, and `test_every_record_this_policy_governs_has_a_stated_rule` is the one that keeps the rule-set honest as records are added. The command reports without changing anything and applies only when asked. THE LEGAL ENTITY PER STOREFRONT: `LegalAcceptanceNamesItsSellingCompanyTest` (8 cases) -- two storefronts name two different selling companies, the receipt FREEZES the identity it was given and a later change never rewrites it, a storefront with no registered company still records its acceptance, and an inactive company is not named. Dormancy and the rollback are pinned too. Re-run by the coordinator on the merged tree: 26 tests / 103 assertions green on MariaDB with the till file below.)
- **(10) Next coding task.** The call is done and in one place; what is left is not the call. In order: (a) drive a GUEST checkout and assert the guest is kept as a hash and the `customer_id` column stays null -- the one claim of this feature that is reasoned rather than measured; (b) a unique key on (order_id, document_type, locale) so write-once is the schema's rather than a test's, which also makes a duplicate impossible instead of merely caught; (c) retention and deletion rules, and the per-storefront legal entity, both of which are owner decisions before they are code. No new switch was added: `gcc.storefronts.legal.explicit_acceptance` ships EMPTY and is already the off state, so a second switch would be two things to turn on and a test matrix for their combination.
- **Open owner decisions.** Which document types need explicit acceptance (none today), whether identity is stored, the retention period, and the per-storefront legal entity (PR 43 body).
- **Verifier note (not applied automatically).** next_task (against remaining item 1): Remaining item 1 says the recorder must run 'inside the order transaction', but next_task says 'after generateOrder'. OrderManager::generateOrder runs all order writes in one DB::transaction (OrderManager.php line 1582, order row inserted at line 1656), so 'after generateOrder' is outside it. The recorder throws a RuntimeException when no effective text exists, which would fail a request whose order is already committed and leave an order without a receipt. Proposed: Call StorefrontLegalAcceptanceRecorder inside generateOrder's transaction right after the order row exists (so the receipt carries the order id and rolls back with the order), for the web and API checkouts, behind a new default-off gcc.storefronts.legal.record_acceptance switch; test that an AE order with explicit_acceptance set to terms stores a row with the order id and an SA order sto

#### I2-06. Legal pages kept separate from the PolicyRegistry operating rules

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 401 | GCC-11; PolicyRegistry docblock; PlatformPolicyController | reviews: none
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Legal policy pages (texts a customer accepts) and the operating rules in PolicyRegistry (numbers and switches the platform applies to itself) are different things, kept in different stores and named differently; neither can change the other.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** origin/main PolicyRegistry holds 12 groups of operating rules (operations, inventory, finance titled payment_terms, analytics and privacy and others) in business_settings through Policy and one admin screen (PlatformPolicyController). It holds no legal text. Legal pages are business_pages. The rules are global, not per country.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** The legal chain keeps the separation by construction: texts and readiness live in storefront_legal_documents and gcc.storefronts.legal, and no GCC pull request touches app/Services/Platform (diff of PR 83 against main is empty). Nothing pins the separation.
- **(5) What remains.** 1) A test that fails if a PolicyRegistry key or group is named for a legal document type, or if readiness reads business_settings -- PR 121 has the name guard, by name only and with eleven live names tolerated. 2) The admin labels are done (2026-11-25): the rules screen is named `operating_rules`, is in the side bar at all for the first time, and says where the legal documents are. What is left of the labels is the group titles INSIDE it (`payment_terms` for payout rules, `analytics_and_privacy`), which the registry declares and whose renaming is the owner's call. 3) A one-line pointer in the PolicyRegistry docblock.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#38](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/38) `122960e`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`; [lavkoo#121](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/121) `af88f045`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `none` No test pins the separation of legal texts from PolicyRegistry
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** None for the pin test. The label part is done and waited for nothing: the legal-document screen (I2-03) already exists at `admin/storefronts/{country}/legal-texts`, and Business Pages has always held the customer-facing texts.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [CODED] Legal texts, versions and readiness are stored and read only through storefront_legal_documents and gcc.storefronts.legal (StorefrontLegalReadiness reads the model and config only; no test pins it)
  - [CODED] PolicyRegistry and Policy are unchanged by the GCC branches (Pull request 121 (draft, base pull request 115's head d5755860, head af88f045, branch gcc/p1-legal-store-pins, TESTS ONLY: five files, all under tests/, no application file changed) pins what the two classes hold on this head and cannot prove a diff. PolicyRegistryLegalSeparationTest (27 tests, 93 assertions, tests/Feature/Storefront) writes the 12 groups of PolicyRegistry::GROUPS and the policy keys of each out by hand and fails, listing what changed, when a group or a policy is added, renamed, moved or removed (its drift reader is itself proved on six in-memory cases); no policy is declared in two groups and definitions() holds one entry per policy; the public members of PolicyRegistry (GROUPS, definition, definitions, has, keysIn) and of Policy (__construct, all, float, get, int, isSet, rules, save) are written out and compared by reflection. CI run 365, the merge of af88f045 into d5755860: "Storefront tests, MySQL 8" and "Storefront tests, MariaDB 11.4.2" each ran phpunit -c phpunit.mysql.xml tests/Unit/Storefront tests/Feature/Storefront, 1250 tests, 4860 assertions, 6 skipped (all AdminStorefrontMenuTest side-bar data sets, SQLite-only by design), no failure; "Tests, SQLite (PHP 8.3)" ran the whole suite, 7539 tests, 797648 assertions, 99 skipped, no failure; this class appears in neither skip list. Auditor's mutations: a policy added to the shipping group fails the drift test, which names it, and Policy::float renamed fails the members test; but changing the type, default, bounds and label of ops_stuck_order_hours, and removing the clamping in Policy::bounded, each leave all 27 green. Why this stays CODED: the pins are reflection over names at this head, not proof that the GCC branches left PolicyRegistry and Policy alone — a test cannot diff a branch against main and the test's own docblock says so (separately, none of the 131 origin/gcc/* and origin/claude/* branches changes a file under app/Services/Platform, or any Policy*.php, against its merge-base with main: a git fact, not a test). Not covered: the definitions of the policies (type, default, bounds, options, label, help), the titles, help and icons of the groups, the order of the groups and the policies, and the code and behaviour of Policy (clamping, fallback to the default, the audit line) and of PasswordPolicy — no test in this pull request or on main exercises Policy.)
  - [CODED] A test fails when a PolicyRegistry key or group is named for a legal document type (Pull request 121 (draft, stacked on pull request 115, base head d5755860, head af88f045, branch gcc/p1-legal-store-pins, TESTS ONLY: five files under tests/, 1,633 added lines, no application file) adds the guard, with eleven names tolerated by name. PolicyRegistryLegalSeparationTest (27 tests, tests/Feature/Storefront) asserts over PolicyRegistry::GROUPS, read as class constants, that no group key and no policy key holds a legal document type as a whole word — terms, privacy, refund, return, cancellation, shipping, cookies, plus whatever gcc.storefronts.legal.required_documents names (today a subset of those, so it adds none; one test sets consumer_rights and requires the check to see a policy named for it) — and, in the other direction, that no legal document type is a policy of the registry (PolicyRegistry::has false, definition null, Policy::get throws InvalidArgumentException for each) and that no policy takes the setting name of a legal business page or of the cookie text (18 names). The name check folds case, camelCase and hyphens and treats a singular and a plural as one word; it is proved on 13 in-memory registries it must name (a policy called terms, privacy_policy, refund_window_days, returnWindow, cancellation-policy, a group called cookies, a type of several words, plurals and singulars) and 4 it must leave alone (returnable_items, refunded_total, terminal_id, shippingless, terms_version against terms_of_service). CI run 365 (pull_request, merge af88f045 into d5755860): Storefront tests, MySQL 8 and Storefront tests, MariaDB 11.4.2 each ran tests/Unit/Storefront tests/Feature/Storefront — 1250 tests, 4860 assertions, 6 skipped (all six AdminStorefrontMenuTest side-bar data sets), no failure; Tests, SQLite (PHP 8.3) ran the whole suite — 7539 tests, 797648 assertions, 99 skipped, no failure; this class appears in neither skip list, and the check reads class constants and configuration only, so no engine is at stake (the strict-mode MariaDB job's path list does not include tests/Feature/Storefront). Auditor's mutations: a policy privacy_notice_days added to the registry fails the guard, which names the key and the type; a group called cookie fails it, which names the group and the type cookies; removing the singular/plural fold fails three provider cases and the registry guard. Why this is CODED and not MET: the judgement is by name only, and eleven live names of the registry do hold a type as a whole word and are tolerated by name with a reason — with the list emptied and the registry untouched the guard reports group:shipping, shipping_silent_hours, shipping_stop_after_days, ops_returns_response_hours, ops_returns_processing_hours and the six health_watch_ and health_at_risk_ cancellation, return and refund rates — so the test fails only for a twelfth name, and the test's docblock leaves the renaming to the owner. Not covered: a policy that holds legal text under a neutral name (the definitions, types and labels are not read), the group titles payment_terms and analytics_and_privacy, which are labels and not checked, and the admin menu naming operating rules and legal documents differently (I2-06.3).)
  - [MET] The admin menu names operating rules and legal documents differently (BUILT, 2026-11-25, and the state it was in was worse than the criterion implies: the operating-rules screen had NO MENU ENTRY AT ALL -- `admin/settings/policies` existed, was behind `module:system_settings` and was reachable only by typing the address -- while calling itself "platform policies", which is exactly what an operator reads as the terms, the privacy notice and the refund policy a customer accepts. #### I2-07. No claim of legal compliance without an independent official review

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 403 | GCC-11; owner charter section 5 (legal review before merge or release); Q-16 | reviews: legal, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** No screen, API field, document or message claims that a country is legally compliant. Readiness means texts are internally approved. A claim of compliance needs an independent official review that the system or the release record can show.

- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#38](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/38) `122960e`; [lavkoo#39](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/39) `8149090`; [lavkoo#43](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/43) `82b6dfa`; [lavkoo#71](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/71) `b83a815`; [lavkoo#105](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/105) `01cefbc1`; [lavkoo#121](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/121) `af88f045`
