ComplianceClaimScanTest (138 tests, tests/Feature/Storefront, extends PHPUnit\Framework\TestCase, no database) reads every PHP and JSON file of every language directory under resources/lang and of each Modules/*/lang, by the key of each string and by its text, and every Blade view under resources/views, resources/themes and Modules/*/resources/views line by line (one test asserts that every directory of resources/lang is read, that over 500 views are read and that four named views are among them, the customer theme footer included), and fails on a phrase that claims compliance, certification or approval by an authority; the phrase list (tests/Support/ComplianceClaimScanner) is proved on 50 sentences that must be flagged and 43 that must not, in English and in Arabic, and on field names (8 that claim, 13 of the readiness answer that do not). Four help sentences of the imported tax template do claim compliance, of the business and not of a country (F-1 to F-4), and are tolerated by name only: tests assert each is still in the language files with exactly the texts on record in every language it has, is shown only by the views listed and only in an admin, vendor, seller or TaxModule staff panel, and is named by no PHP file of app/, Modules/, routes/ or config/; 13 data sets prove that a reworded text, a text reworded in another language, an extra claim, another key or an unlisted view is not tolerated, so the list can only shrink. ComplianceClaimPayloadsTest (9 tests) builds six storefronts through the real services and scans the field names and the texts of four answers: GET /api/v1/storefronts (whose sellable and state per country are pinned first, so the scan is of the world the test describes), the 422 refusal (code storefront_not_sellable) of a payment started through the real customer payment route in each of the four storefronts that may not sell, the 503 refusal of a probe route the test registers with the real storefront middleware pair (the same pair guards the cart, product and theme API groups), and what StorefrontAdminBoard::rows() reads; the refusals are asserted to say 'not open for checkout' and 'under maintenance', the same two texts are pinned in English and in Arabic in the language files, and one test adds a claim to a real answer and requires the scan to name it. StorefrontLegalReview keeps who reviewed a storefront's legal texts, where the opinion is written and the day, with an audit line in the same transaction. gcc:storefront:legal-review records, reports and withdraws it. record() refuses a tool or an assistant as reviewer (best effort on free text), no source, a day that is not YYYY-MM-DD and a day in the future. A stored row that does not say all of it counts for nothing, but a stored row with a future day is not re-checked. A job proves a test only when its path list holds the directory of that test: the strict-mode job's list holds neither tests/Feature/Storefront nor tests/Feature/Address, so what runs in those directories has run under strict mode only locally. The pull request's own run is not read)
  - [MET] A test scans language files, views and API payloads for compliance claims (Pull request 121 (draft, branch gcc/p1-legal-store-pins, head af88f045, stacked on pull request 115 at d5755860, TESTS ONLY: five files under tests/, no application file) is the guard. ComplianceClaimScanTest (138 tests, tests/Feature/Storefront, no database) scans the language files — every PHP and JSON file of all seven directories under resources/lang (ae, bd, en, es, in, ks, sa) and of Modules/TaxModule/lang, by the key of each string and by its text — and the views: every Blade file under resources/views, resources/themes and Modules/*/resources/views, line by line and then whole-file for a sentence broken across lines (1,556 files today; the test asserts more than 500 are read, that every language directory is read, and that four named views including the customer theme footer are read). ComplianceClaimPayloadsTest (9 tests) scans four answers, field names and texts: GET /api/v1/storefronts, the 422 refusal of POST api/v1/digital-payment through the real route behind the real sale gate in each of the four storefronts that may not sell (KW, QA, BH, OM), the 503 maintenance refusal of the real middleware for BH on a probe route the test registers, and what StorefrontAdminBoard::rows() gives the admin screen (a service answer, not HTTP); it first pins the state and sellability of all six storefronts, so a scan of the wrong answer cannot pass. Both assert that the findings no entry tolerates are none, printing file, line, key, claims and text; the four existing claims (the imported tax-template help of the admin and seller panels) are tolerated only by key, text, claim list and listed view, and every listed view must be a staff panel, so no customer-facing claim can be tolerated. The phrase list (tests/Support/ComplianceClaimScanner) is proved on 50 sentences that must be flagged (26 English, 24 Arabic) and 43 that must not (27 English, 16 Arabic), on 8 field names that claim and the 13 field names of the readiness answers that do not, on 13 data sets that keep a tolerance from stretching to a reworded text, another language, an extra claim, another key or an unlisted view, and the walkers are proved on a fixture tree (nested array, Arabic file, JSON file, module language directory, a claim broken over two lines; a .bak file and a non-Blade file are not read). #### I2-09. Sale gate on POS, seller API and marketplace order writers

- **State: NOT STARTED** | plan lines 395 | GCC-11; handoff 2026-11-15 (POS where relevant); PR 49 body; storefront flag runbook | reviews: owner-decision, security
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Order creation that does not come through the customer routes (admin, vendor and seller POS, the marketplace order materializer, other order writers) either refuses a storefront that may not sell or is recorded as a deliberate exception, so no order-starting path is missed.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** nothing. The gate exists only as middleware on 12 customer routes. The admin, vendor and seller v3 POS order paths and ChannelOrderMaterializer carry no gate and have no storefront context.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** nothing is built. The gap is named in the PR 42 and PR 49 bodies and in the flag runbook ("The seller and admin POS, the marketplace order materializer and the sales-channel webhooks are not behind the sale gate"). The only generateOrder callers are the gated customer routes and the payment-success hook.
- **(5) What remains.** 1) Owner decision: is a POS or marketplace sale a storefront sale. 2) If yes: derive the storefront from the products' country and call StorefrontSellingGate in the POS order paths and the materializer. 3) A source-scan test listing every order writer so a new one cannot skip the gate. 4) The seller storefront context (seller app PRs 1 and 2).
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#42](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/42) `2534953`; [lavkoo#49](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/49) `b52d9b2`; [lavkoo#117](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/117) `7a638ed1`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `none` No test pins that POS and materializer orders are ungated today
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Owner decision on POS and marketplace orders. Needs the seller storefront context (lavkooappseller PR 1 and 2, drafts). Marketplace orders may be obligations that must keep flowing during a stop.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] A POS sale (admin, vendor, seller v3) in a storefront that may not sell is refused (**RAISED MISSING -> MET, 2026-11-26. The card said NOT STARTED while the work was done and ON.** `PointOfSaleSaleRule` is wired into ALL THREE tills the criterion names -- `Admin\POS\POSOrderController`, `Vendor\POS\POSOrderController` and `RestAPI\v3\seller\POSController` -- through `OrderCreatorRegister`, and its switch `gcc.sale_gate.at_the_till` is on by default (C-16). `TheTillOfAStoppedMarketSellsNothingTest` (10 cases) carries the refusal for BOTH states a market can be stopped in: one nobody has made active and one a person has paused, each writing nothing. It also pins what must NOT be refused, which is where a gate of this kind does its damage: the default market is not refused for being the default, goods that name no market sell as they do today, goods of a market the configuration does not name still sell, and before the market column exists the till sells as it always did. Two dormancy cases besides. Re-run by the coordinator on the merged tree: green.)

#### I2-11. Store lifecycle states Draft, Ready, Active, Paused, managed by admin

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 447-449 | GCC-12; GCC-19; GCC-20 document level 3; Q-20; R-057 | reviews: security, owner-decision

#### I2-14. Stop mode Maintenance per storefront

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 453 | GCC-12; R-057; E06 sections 2.7 and findings F02, F03; Q-40 | reviews: owner-decision, native-arabic, security
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A single storefront can show a maintenance page while the other storefronts keep selling. Order tracking, the customer's orders, refunds, payments in flight, callbacks, webhooks and admin stay reachable, and the state survives a cache clear.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** main has one platform-wide maintenance mode (five systems, a window, a message): the maintenance_mode middleware on the web groups and the vendor and seller panels, with the state kept only in the cache key system_maintenance_mode; GET config returns maintenance_status. It blocks the web storefront including my orders and tracking (E06). No test pins it.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** nothing.
- **(5) What remains.** 1) A per-storefront maintenance state in the database (I2-11) with the cache rebuilt from it. 2) A web middleware on the storefront group with exemptions for payment, callback, webhook, order-tracking, customer-order and refund routes and the admin guard. 3) A per-storefront message and window, also in the config answer for the apps. 4) Tests including a cache clear.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#111](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/111) `d8f6a7ea`; [lavkoo#119](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/119) `71a05e62`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `none` No test covers the existing maintenance mode or a per-store one
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Needs I2-11. Reuse the exclusion list of ApplySeoRedirects. The owner answers Q-40: whether order, refund and support pages stay reachable in a stop.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**

#### I2-15. Stop mode Redirect to the default storefront

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 455 | GCC-12; Q-20; flag runbook "Switching a country off" | reviews: owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** A stopped storefront can send its visitors to the default storefront: web page requests redirect to the equivalent default-storefront page, the apps move to the default, and payments, callbacks, webhooks, admin and the API contract are never redirected.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** main has an admin-managed redirects table and the ApplySeoRedirects middleware (301 or 302 rules; it excludes /api, /admin, /vendor, /install, /update, /payment). No storefront redirect.
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** Implicit fallback only (PR 19): a request that asks for an inactive country is served the default storefront, with none of the other country's products, and the runbook calls deactivating a country the kill switch (pinned by StorefrontKillSwitchTest, PR 53). It is a silent serve, not an explicit redirect or a chosen mode, and the customer app keeps an unlisted stored choice as a browse-only storefront.
- **(5) What remains.** 1) An explicit redirect mode in the state model (I2-11): a 302 to the same page on the default storefront for GET web requests only. 2) Exemptions for POST, payment, callback, webhook, admin and API. 3) App: reset a stored storefront the server no longer lists and tell the customer. 4) The owner picks 301 or 302 and the SEO effect.
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#19](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/19) `74372eb`; [lavkoo#53](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/53) `7e32bba`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`; [lavkooapp#5](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkooapp/pull/5) `5526c4b`; [lavkoo#111](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/111) `d8f6a7ea`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` StorefrontKillSwitchTest (3) and StorefrontResolverTest (30) (tests/Feature/Storefront/StorefrontKillSwitchTest.php, tests/Unit/Storefront/StorefrontResolverTest.php; PR 83 head 8ff3410: Tests SQLite PHP 8.2 and 8.3, Storefront tests MySQL 8, Storefront tests MariaDB 11.4.2 (all success))
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Needs I2-11. Reuse the ApplySeoRedirects exclusions. The app part touches storefront_config.dart on the lavkooapp PR 5 branch.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] A request asking for an inactive country is served the default storefront and none of that country's products (StorefrontKillSwitchTest test_a_visitor_who_asks_for_a_deactivated_country_is_served_the_default_storefront_and_none_of_its_products; PR 83)
  - [MISSING] A stopped storefront in mode Redirect answers a web GET with a redirect to the same page on the default storefront (PR 111 (d8f6a7ea): redirect is a stop mode that is stored, audited and listed with redirect_to (the default storefront), but the website resolves no storefront from a request yet (no route group under a country prefix), so no web GET is redirected)
  - [MISSING] The redirect never applies to POST, payment, callback, webhook, admin and API requests
  - [MISSING] The customer app resets a stored storefront that the server no longer lists and tells the customer (StorefrontConfig.resolve falls back only for a code the app does not know)
  - [MET] The mode is selectable per country and audited (PR 111 (d8f6a7ea): the mode is chosen per country by the person who pauses it, stored and audited with who, from, to and why (StorefrontLifecycleTest). CI run 342 (the all-in-one integration, head a5f7de59, which holds this pull request's code), read per job on 2026-11-18: 'Tests, SQLite' on PHP 8.2 and on PHP 8.3 (the whole suite), the Storefront, Address, Catalog, Staff scope, Checkout/payment/permission, Fulfillment + security, Saudi baseline and Wallet/Loyalty groups on MySQL 8.0 and on MariaDB 11.4.2 and the strict-mode job on MariaDB, and the five sales-channel and cost groups on MySQL 8.0 and MariaDB 11.4.2 (the last to finish, group A to M on MySQL 8.0, at 16:46 UTC): success (32 jobs: 30 success, 2 skipped by design, the deployment overlay and the Saudi baseline expectations check). A job proves a test only when its path list holds the directory of that test: the strict-mode job's list holds neither tests/Feature/Storefront nor tests/Feature/Address, so what runs in those directories has run under strict mode only locally. The pull request's own run is not read)
- **(10) Next coding task.** After I2-11: in the web storefront group add a redirect step for paused countries in mode redirect (GET only, 302, same path on the default storefront, exemptions copied from ApplySeoRedirects::EXCLUDED_PREFIXES plus the webhook paths) with a test matrix; in lavkooapp PR 5's branch make StorefrontConfig.resolve fall back to the default when the stored code is absent from the stored server list.
- **Open owner decisions.** Whether the redirect is 301 or 302, whether apps are moved automatically, and the default stop mode (Q-20).

#### I2-16. Stop never loses earlier orders or breaks webhooks, refunds, settlements, shipments, support

- **State: IN PROGRESS** | plan lines 457 | GCC-12; Q-40; R-057; handoff 2026-11-15 (refunds, cancellation, support, callbacks and old obligations continue) | reviews: finance, security, owner-decision
- **(2) Functional requirement.** Stopping a storefront never loses earlier orders and never breaks webhooks, refunds, settlements, shipments or support for them: payments in flight finish in their own storefront, refund, cancel and due-payment routes stay open, and background jobs are not narrowed by the stop.
- **(3) Exists now in code (main).** main has no storefront stop. Payment callbacks, gateway pages, webhooks and the whole REST API sit outside the maintenance_mode groups (E06 section 2.7); the web maintenance does block my orders, tracking, invoice, refund and support pages (R-057).
- **(4) Done in the draft PRs.** PR 42 and 49: refund request and store, cancel, the four due-payment routes and the MyFatoorah status check carry no sale gate (pinned by a route list). PR 53: a payment started in a storefront is finished in it even if the storefront was deactivated or removed since (PaymentStorefront, 16 tests). Orders carry no country and are not narrowed (runbook), so earlier orders stay readable; webhook routes sit outside the storefront middleware.
- **(5) What remains.** 1) A continuity matrix test per stop state: refund, cancel, due payments, status check, webhooks, order tracking, customer order list, admin order screens, settlement and shipment jobs keep working. 2) Pin the webhook routes as exempt. 3) Settlement, shipment and support flows are untested against a stopped country (no stop mode exists yet). 4) Decide the order and refund pages during maintenance (Q-40).
- **(6) PRs and commits.** [lavkoo#42](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/42) `2534953`; [lavkoo#49](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/49) `b52d9b2`; [lavkoo#53](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/53) `7e32bba`; [lavkoo#83](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/83) `8ff3410`; [lavkoo#111](https://github.com/fullstackfull/lavkoo/pull/111) `7e19d284`
- **(7) Tests.**
  - `ci` StorefrontSalesRoutesTest (open actions stay open), PaymentStorefrontTest (16), StorefrontKillSwitchTest (3) (tests/Feature/Storefront/; PR 83 head 8ff3410: Tests SQLite PHP 8.2 and 8.3, Storefront tests MySQL 8, Storefront tests MariaDB 11.4.2 (all success); also PR 53 head 7e32bba Storefront tests MySQL 8 and MariaDB 11.4.2)
- **(8) Dependencies and blockers.** Cannot be finished before the stop modes exist (I2-11, I2-13 to I2-15). Settlement and shipment flows belong to the finance and fulfillment units. The gate files are the same shared files as I2-08, so no combined-tree proof exists.
- **(9) Acceptance criteria.**
  - [MET] Refund request and store, order cancel, the four due-payment routes and the MyFatoorah status check carry no sale gate (StorefrontSalesRoutesTest test_refunds_cancellations_callbacks_and_obligations_on_placed_orders_stay_open; PR 83)
  - [MET] A payment started in a storefront is finished in that storefront even if it was switched off or removed since (PaymentStorefrontTest test_a_storefront_switched_off_since_the_payment_started_is_still_the_one_it_is_finished_in; PR 53 7e32bba and PR 83)
  - [CODED] Earlier orders stay readable and manageable after their storefront is deactivated (PR 111 (draft, stacked on PR 105, 7e19d284): through the API routes of the apps, for an order that the real checkout of the API placed before a stop, in each stop mode (browse only, maintenance and redirect on Kuwait, a non-default storefront; browse only on the default storefront) the order is still listed (the one order, with the storefront it was sold in), its line is returned (order, product and quantity; its prices, tax, product details and seller are not asserted), it is tracked (its status and the first two steps of its timeline, placed reached and confirmed not; the later steps are not asserted), a pending cash-on-delivery order is cancelled (order and line status, the unit back in stock), a refund is asked for (the refund data is read and the request is stored as pending; a decision on it is not driven), the customer replies to a support ticket, reads its conversation, writes a chat message and reads it (the ticket is created before the stop; the ticket and the chat belong to the customer, not to the order), and the due payment is set to cash on delivery or to an offline transfer (the method, the note and the transfer reference are stored; no payment is taken); the same answers come without a stop on each of those two storefronts (the controls), and a new sale is refused in the four stopped cases and taken in the two controls (StorefrontStopContinuityTest: five of its ten driven methods, each run six ways, 30 of its 65 tests). Not driven, so the criterion is CODED, not MET: a storefront switched off in the configuration (active false), which is what the runbook and StorefrontKillSwitchTest call deactivating a country, is not driven by this class (only a payment started in one is driven, M2-14.1), so the sentence is proven here for a storefront paused in each of the three modes only; the website's own order pages and actions and the staff side of an order (status, delivery, refund decision) are checked only in the route table to carry neither the sale gate nor the maintenance gate (structural). CI: run 355 (id 44412761889) on the merge of 7e19d284 into its base 01cefbc1, an ancestor of the head, so the tree is the head's, read per job on 2026-11-18: 'Tests, SQLite' on PHP 8.2 and on PHP 8.3 (the whole suite: 7,230 tests, 99 skipped, none of them in StorefrontStopContinuityTest) and 'Storefront tests' on MySQL 8 and on MariaDB 11.4.2 (941 tests, 4,660 assertions, none skipped): success, so all 65 tests of the class ran and passed in the four jobs; 22 of the 23 jobs are success and the deployment overlay is red by design. The strict-mode MariaDB job is success but does not run tests/Feature/Storefront; under a strict SQL server the 60 driven cases of the class skip until PR 89 (only its five route-table tests run), so no driven case has run on a strict server at this head.)
  - [CODED] Gateway callbacks and courier or marketplace webhooks are outside the storefront and sale middleware (PR 111 (7e19d284): the two stop gates are proven absent from the gateway, courier and marketplace routes; the storefront resolver, which the sentence also names, is not proven. Driven (StorefrontStopContinuityTest: 65 tests, ten driven tests of six cases each and five route-table tests; the six cases are browse only, maintenance and redirect on Kuwait, browse only on the default storefront, and no stop on each of those two as the controls): the MyFatoorah webhook (signed: it settles a payment started before the stop and makes its order after it; repeated after the stop: it answers 'duplicate' and makes no second order), the return of the customer (GET payment/myfatoorah/callback redirects to the success page) and the Torod webhook (it stores the courier status of a shipment of an earlier order; the stored notice is applied by the worker step, called directly; the shipment row is inserted by the test) are answered as before in every stop mode, and a new sale is refused in the stopped cases and taken in the controls. These requests carry no storefront, as a gateway's do, so the driven cases do not by themselves show that the routes carry no gate: a sale gate added behind the resolver is seen by them only in the default storefront's browse-only case, and a maintenance gate by none (the default storefront cannot be put in maintenance). Structural (the three route-table tests; proved by mutation: a gate added to a webhook route or to the website's MyFatoorah routes, or spelled by class with a parameter, fails them): no route under payment/ (49 routes), api/v1/payment/, api/myfatoorah/, api/fulfillment-webhooks/, api/sales-channel-webhooks/ or api/delivery-syria/ (54 routes in all) carries the sale gate (storefront.sellable) or the maintenance gate (storefront.available), and over the whole route table the sale gate sits on the ten new-sale actions only and the maintenance gate on the cart, catalogue and theme routes only, however a gate is declared on a route (alias, class, parameter or group); a gate declared in a controller constructor is not seen. The four routes that start a payment (api/v1/digital-payment, api/v1/add-to-fund, customer/web-payment-request, customer/customer-add-fund-request) carry the sale gate by design. Not proven: 'storefront middleware' is, in the code, the resolver (alias 'storefront', ResolveStorefront). The route table shows the routes under api/myfatoorah/, api/fulfillment-webhooks/, api/sales-channel-webhooks/ and api/delivery-syria/ without it, but no test asserts that (the resolver added to the MyFatoorah webhook route leaves all 65 tests green), and the website's payment pages and callbacks, the 49 routes under payment/ among them the return of the customer, do run it: it binds the request's storefront, the default one for a gateway's request, and applies no stop. The criterion is therefore CODED until the owner says whether it means the resolver or the two stop gates; read as the two stop gates, the tests above prove it. The other gateways and couriers (a whole address family each) and the marketplace webhooks are structural only, not driven. PR 38: SA is grandfathered in legal readiness. Changing the default is an environment edit with no readiness or pause check and no audit.
